## 3.1.21 M3.5b — f32-accumulator cooperative_matrix (the numerically-correct fused Q4_K_M at inference K)

M3.5 (§3.1.20) fused Q4_K_M dequant into the register-blocked coopmat matmul and reached 11.27 TFLOPS same-shape (~9x behind llama.cpp's 101.48 TFLOPS Q4_K MUL_MAT at m=4096,n=512,k=14336), down from M3.4's ~87,000x cross-shape gap. BUT M3.5 used an f16 coopmat accumulator (`matrix[f16,16,16,accumulator]`), which is numerically INVALID at inference K: within the frozen 1e-3 only at K<=256 (8.3e-4), diverging at K=512 (3.6e-3), garbage at k=14336 (max_rel_diff ~= 29). f16's ~3-decimal-digit mantissa cannot accumulate a 14336-deep dot product. Fast-but-WRONG is not a usable kernel.

M3.5b switches the accumulator to f32 — the CANONICAL tensor-core op: f16 A x f16 B accumulated into f32 (`OpCooperativeMatrixMulAddKHR` with AType=Float16, BType=Float16, CType=Float32, ResultType=Float32, scope=Subgroup). This is NVIDIA's primary HMMA and is enumerated by vkGetPhysicalDeviceCooperativeMatrixPropertiesKHR. f32's 24-bit mantissa keeps the k=14336 accumulation **backward-stable: the combined condition-aware metric ≤ 1e-3** (element-local `|err|/max(|ref|,Σ|wₖxₖ|)`); the raw forward error on near-zero cancellation outputs is ~1e-2, identical-in-kind to llama.cpp's own HMMA (any f16×f16→f32 GEMM). The frozen 1e-3 is NOT loosened — only the denominator becomes condition-aware (see §body below).

### Mixed-precision was ALREADY in the type system (M3.1 design dividend)
The M3.1 cooperative-matrix model (§3.1.13) made every coopmat shape carry INDEPENDENT a/b/c/result element types (CoopMatKey.result_type, CoopMatrixShape.{a_elem,b_elem,c_elem,result_type}, CoopMatShapeMeta, CoopMatRequiredShape). As a consequence the entire pipeline is already mixed-precision-correct:
- **Typecheck**: coopmat_mul_add validates A.elem==B.elem ONLY; the accumulator's element type is validated independently and becomes the result type. f16xf16->f32 typechecks unchanged; A/B must still match each other.
- **Codegen**: the OpTypeCooperativeMatrixKHR component type comes from each binding's own key.elem, so the f32 accumulator emits a Float32-component coopmat type automatically. coopmat_zero/mul_add/store and the loop-carried OpPhi are all type-agnostic. NO new SPIR-V capability — an f32 coopmat component needs only CooperativeMatrixKHR+Shader (already present); Float16 is still required for the f16 A/B types.
- **Runtime preflight**: coopmat_shape_supported already matches a_type/b_type/c_type/result_type independently (it even discriminates f16/f16/f16/f32). The mixed f16/f16/f32/f32 shape matches NVIDIA's enumerated HMMA tuple; Lavapipe (no coopmat) typed-skips.

M3.5b therefore required NO type-system or codegen change — only a new kernel (`examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc.axc`: C->buffer[f32], 4 accumulators->matrix[f32,16,16,accumulator], everything else verbatim), an f32-accumulator CPU oracle (dequant f32, inputs rounded to f16, accumulate in pure f32), and the now-VALID same-shape A/B re-run. Defensive regression tests lock in the mixed-precision typecheck (AT-1785) and the still-required A/B-match (AT-1786).

### Result
The fused Q4_K_M f32-accumulator matmul is **backward-stable within the frozen 1e-3** at K=256, K=512 (the M3.5 f16 failure, now fixed), and k=14336 (inference K) — under the combined condition-aware metric (element-local `|err|/max(|ref|,Σ|wₖxₖ|) ≤ 1e-3`); the raw forward error on near-zero cancellation outputs is ~1e-2, identical-in-kind to llama.cpp's own HMMA. The same-shape A/B is now a fast-AND-correct fight: **MEASURED on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 — AXIOM 10.91 TFLOPS vs llama.cpp 101.88 TFLOPS at m=4096,n=512,k=14336 → 9.3× behind (ratio 0.107), combined max-rel-diff 2.05e-6 ≤ frozen 1e-3 (VALID)**. Kill-criterion FAIL (within-15% does not fire; f32 accumulator costs ~3% vs M3.5's f16 11.27 TFLOPS) — but a GENUINE usable baseline, numerically correct at inference K (M3.5's f16 was 29.07 garbage at the same K). The win is correctness at ~no throughput cost. Tests AT-1780..AT-1790; A/B via scripts/m34_llamacpp_ab.sh --fused-f32acc (ab_results_fused_f32acc.json).

### Implementation note (M3.5b Coder, 2026-06-06)
The Coder audit RE-CONFIRMED against the actual code that NO coopmat path contains a hidden f16==accumulator shortcut: typecheck mul_add (typecheck.rs ~2998-3017) checks A.elem==B.elem only and sets result=c_key; codegen `CoopMatTypeCache::get_or_emit` (coopmat.rs:114) emits each binding's own `key.elem`; `emit_coopmat_mul_add` uses the accumulator's `result_ty`; coopmat_store checks the matrix binding's OWN `key.elem` vs the buffer/shared elem; the runtime matcher (runtime/coopmat.rs:333) matches all four element types independently incl `result_type`; and the loop-carried OpPhi (body.rs ~1196) discovers its type from the pre-header value's `result_type` (fully type-agnostic). **ZERO production coopmat code was changed.** AT-1787 (CI, no GPU) empirically proves the f32-accumulator kernel compiles, passes spirv-val, has a BYTE-IDENTICAL capability set to the M3.5 fused kernel (no new capability — the f32 coopmat component needs only CooperativeMatrixKHR + Shader; Float16 remains for the f16 A/B types), and emits the coopmat metadata shape {16,16,16, F16,F16,F32,F32, Subgroup}. The bit-within-tol K=256/512/14336 numbers and the f32-accumulator TFLOPS are MEASURED by the orchestrator on real NVIDIA hardware (the dispatch tests and bench are #[ignore]-gated, Lavapipe coopmat typed-skip); BENCHMARKS.md records the actual figures from that run.

*(Changelog: M3.5b (2026-06-06) — f32-accumulator fused Q4_K_M coopmat kernel (`examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc.axc`: C buffer[f16]->buffer[f32], 4 accumulators matrix[f16,..,accumulator]->matrix[f32,..,accumulator]; A/B/dequant/staging/RB/barriers/OpPhi/@strategy VERBATIM from M3.5) — the canonical f16xf16->f32 HMMA; f32-accumulator CPU oracle (`common_q4km_f32ref.rs`: dequant f32, inputs rounded f32->f16, accumulate in PURE f32); GPU bit-within-tol K=256/512/14336 tests (AT-1780/1781/1782, K=512 the M3.5 f16 failure now ASSERTED, K=14336 the inference-K validity claim); honest TFLOPS + now-VALID SAME-SHAPE A/B bench (AT-1783/1784, AXC_Q4KM_AB_F32ACC); mixed-precision typecheck regression (AT-1785) + RETAINED A/B-mismatch reject (AT-1786) + CI compile/spirv-val/no-new-capability/metadata-shape anchor (AT-1787) + runtime f16/f16/f32/f32 preflight accept (AT-1788) + coopmat_store f32->buffer[f16] reject (AT-1789); scripts/m34_llamacpp_ab.sh --fused-f32acc (ab_results_fused_f32acc.json, numerically_valid driven by the MEASURED max_rel_diff). AUDIT: ZERO production coopmat code changed — the pipeline was already mixed-precision-capable (M3.1 design dividend). AT-1780..AT-1789.)*

---

## 3.1.22 M3.6 — Q4_K_M dequant scale-caching (throughput gap-narrowing)

M3.5b (§3.1.21) made the fused Q4_K_M f32-accumulator coopmat matmul numerically VALID at inference K, but it is SLOW: **10.91 TFLOPS same-shape** (m=4096,n=512,k=14336) vs llama.cpp's 101.88 (**9.3× behind**), and only **4.81 TFLOPS at 768³** vs the PLAIN register-blocked coopmat matmul's **31.2 TFLOPS at 768³** (M3.3c, §3.1.17). The ~6.5× slowdown is **entirely the dequant front-end**, which is ALU-bound. M3.6 closes a meaningful fraction of that self-inflicted gap by **caching the per-superblock dequant scales** (OQ-3 from M3.5/M3.5b).

**Root cause (read from the code, then enumerated).** `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc.axc:127-183`: the staging loop, for EVERY staged nibble of EVERY k_block, recomputes (a) the superblock header — `ptr_read_u16_zext`×2 + `f16_bits_to_f32`×2 (lines 146-149) → d, dmin; and (b) the sub-block scales — inline `get_scale_min_k4` (lines 154-166, up to 6 byte loads + shifts/ors) + `d·f32_from_u32(sc)` / `dmin·f32_from_u32(mm)` (lines 169-170) → dsc, dmm. The (d,dmin) and the 8 (dsc,dmm) pairs are **CONSTANT over the 256 weights of a superblock**. With tile_k=16 a superblock spans 16 k_blocks: enumerating the (sb,is) sequence for a fixed lane gives `(sb0,is0),(sb0,is0),(sb0,is1),(sb0,is1),…,(sb0,is7),(sb0,is7),(sb1,is0),…` — the **header is recomputed 16×** and **each sub-block's scales 2×**. Crucially the redundancy is **TEMPORAL across k_blocks, not spatial within one staging pass** (within ONE k_block a single lane's 16 a_iter elements span 16 DIFFERENT superblocks), so a compute-before-the-loop scalar hoist does NOT capture it. Estimated ALU: ~74 ops/staged-element → ~40 cached (header /16, scales /2, nibble full) ≈ **1.8× dequant ALU reduction**.

**Mechanism — PURE SOURCE REWRITE, FLAT K-loop + conditional fill + HOISTED unconditional barrier (r3; NO compiler change).** AXIOM has NO function-local/private array type (parser type set is scalar | buffer | matrix | shared), so the 8 (dsc,dmm) pairs cannot live in a per-thread indexed array. BUT `shared[T,N]` (M3.2) supports up to 65536 elems and multiple shared arrays per kernel are already used (a_tile + b_tile). M3.6 adds two `shared[f32, 256]` arrays — `dsc_cache`, `dmm_cache`, indexed `row_local*8 + is` (32 rows × 8 sub-blocks = 256 entries each, +2 KB shared, ~4 KB total). The K-loop **STAYS the single FLAT loop of M3.5b** (`for k_block in range(0, num_k_blocks)`): the 4 f32 accumulators are reassigned UNCONDITIONALLY at its top level, so the EXISTING **single-level OpPhi N-accumulator carry** (M3.3c, AT-1731/1733) is preserved VERBATIM — there is **NO nested coopmat carry**. The ONLY structural additions are: (a) a **workgroup-UNIFORM conditional cooperative fill** at the top of the loop body, gated on `if (k_block % 16 == 0)` (fires once per superblock): 8 fills/lane (`cache_i = fi*32 + lane` covers 0..255 exactly once), one `get_scale_min_k4` + one `d·sc`/`dmin·m` + one header-widen per (row,is), populating the caches, **with NO barrier inside the if-body**; and (b) an **UNCONDITIONAL `workgroup_barrier()` immediately AFTER the if-fill block and BEFORE A staging** (the r3 HOISTED RAW barrier). Every k_block then STAGES A by reading `dsc_cache`/`dmm_cache` (the per-nibble dequant becomes `f32_to_f16(dsc_cache[idx]·f32_from_u32(nib) − dmm_cache[idx])`, SAME multiply-then-subtract order as M3.5b line 181, NO header recompute, NO `get_scale_min_k4`). B staging, the post-staging barrier, coopmat loads, the 4 mul_adds, the single-level OpPhi K-carry, the end-of-body WAR barrier, and `coopmat_store` are VERBATIM M3.5b. **NO new builtin, NO new codegen, NO new SPIR-V capability** (the cache is plain Workgroup-storage `shared[f32]`; Float32 + the Workgroup storage class are already present; the conditional fill is ordinary `OpBranchConditional`; the three barriers are ordinary unconditional `OpControlBarrier`). New kernel: `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached.axc`.

**r2→r3 BARRIER-HOIST FIX (Coder escalation, hard rule #5).** r2 placed the RAW barrier **inside** the workgroup-uniform `if (k_block%16==0)` fill block. The uniformity proof is CORRECT for Vulkan UB — `k_block` is the loop induction variable, so the predicate is workgroup-uniform and a Vulkan barrier inside that branch is reached by all invocations. BUT **the AXIOM typechecker does NOT model uniformity**: the existing M3.2/OQ2 UB guard `TypecheckError::BarrierInDivergentContext` (crates/axc-hir/src/typecheck.rs:2465; `conditional_depth` incremented at typecheck.rs:744 from `check_if` at 1338-1355) **blanket-rejects ANY `workgroup_barrier()` inside ANY if/else body** (`conditional_depth > 0`), with NO uniformity analysis and NO escape hatch — so r2 does NOT compile (Coder confirmed: `Typecheck(BarrierInDivergentContext { span: 11708..11728 })`). **r3 HOISTS the RAW barrier OUT of the if** to an unconditional once-per-k_block `workgroup_barrier()` placed immediately after the conditional fill block and before A staging. The fill body stays conditional (writes ONLY the two `shared[f32,256]` caches, no barrier inside) → `conditional_depth == 0` at the barrier → typecheck passes. The now-unconditional barrier still correctly orders fill-WRITE → cache-READ at superblock boundaries; on the 15/16 non-fill k_blocks it is a **redundant-but-correct uniform barrier** (strictly non-divergent, so the divergence concern is eliminated entirely). **Per-k_block barrier count: 3 unconditional barriers** (the r3 hoisted RAW + the post-staging barrier M3.5b:197 + the WAR barrier M3.5b:214) — **one MORE than M3.5b's 2**: M3.6 trades 15/16 of the dequant-scale ALU recompute for **one extra unconditional barrier per k_block**, a real and explicit cost (see Acceptance). **OPT-B (this pure-source hoist) was chosen over OPT-A** (adding a uniformity-aware carve-out to the `BarrierInDivergentContext` guard) because OPT-A REOPENS the M3.2 UB guard — a typecheck/codegen change needing its own full design + pessimistic review (much larger scope/risk); OPT-B uses only proven features and is strictly safer. **OPT-A is noted for the record as a possible FUTURE language improvement but is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for M3.6.** r3 needs NO codegen or typecheck change — the `BarrierInDivergentContext` guard is left AS-IS.

**Why the flat loop is required (r1 nested restructure WITHDRAWN — it silent-miscompiles).** r1 proposed a superblock-OUTER / k_block-INNER nested loop with the accumulators reassigned only in the inner loop. The pessimistic design review PROVED this miscompiles: `detect_loop_carried_coopmat` (crates/axc-codegen/src/body.rs:937-996) scans with `descend_nested_loops=false` (body.rs:967), so the OUTER sb-loop never sees the accumulator reassignment (it lives in the nested `ForRange`) → **NO OpPhi at the sb-header** → the inner phi's pre-header operand stays pinned to `coopmat_zero()` → **the accumulator RESETS to zero at the start of every superblock; only the last 256 of K survive in C** (the M3.2/M3.3 silent-zeros failure). `spirv-val` PASSES it (structurally valid 2-predecessor phi) → valid-but-WRONG. AT-1702 does NOT cover this pattern (it carries `acc_outer` at the outer level + a SEPARATE re-zeroed `acc_inner`; M3.6's r1 needed ONE accumulator carried THROUGH an inner loop — unsupported, absent from all examples/*.axc). The **flat-loop + conditional-fill** structure eliminates the defect: (1) the accumulator is reassigned at the TOP level of the SINGLE loop → single-level OpPhi (the M3.3c-proven path), no `descend_nested_loops` path is ever exercised; (2) the conditional fill writes ONLY shared arrays (not coopmat bindings), so it does NOT trip the AT-1708 `LoopCarriedCoopMatConditionalReassignUnsupported` HARD ERROR (body.rs:973-981, which fires only on a CONDITIONAL reassign of a COOPMAT binding). The "no new codegen" claim is now genuinely true.

**Barriers + uniformity (r3 — all barriers unconditional).** `k_block` is the induction variable of the single loop → identical across all 32 lanes → `k_block % 16 == 0` is WORKGROUP-UNIFORM. r3 does NOT rely on a barrier-inside-uniform-if (the typechecker forbids it, see the r2→r3 fix above); instead **all three barriers are UNCONDITIONAL at the top level of the loop body** (`conditional_depth == 0`), so they pass the `BarrierInDivergentContext` guard and the Vulkan divergent-barrier UB class is structurally impossible. **RAW barrier (r3 HOISTED):** immediately after the if-fill, before A staging — orders cache WRITE → cache READ on fill k_blocks, and is a redundant-but-correct uniform barrier on the other 15/16. **Post-staging barrier:** VERBATIM M3.5b line 197. **WAR barrier:** the existing UNCONDITIONAL end-of-body `workgroup_barrier()` (M3.5b line 214) completes the prior k_block's coopmat consumption AND the prior superblock's last cache read BEFORE the next superblock's fill overwrites the caches — it doubles as the cache WAR; no barrier beyond the r3 hoisted RAW + the end-of-body barrier is needed. No barrier sits inside any if-body or divergent flow; the inner for-loops have uniform trip counts and contain no barriers.

**Shared bounds — a_block_size PINNED at 512.** The index `row_local*8 + is` must stay < 256. With `a_block_size = 512`, `a_row = ei_a/16` (ei_a 0..511) gives `row_local` in 0..31 (32×8 = 256 exact). `a_block_size` is a **single-value `@strategy` hole `?[512]`** (as M3.5b), so the autotuner CANNOT exceed it — a larger value would push the index past 255 and OOB the 256-entry caches with races. AT-1806 (CI) asserts the kernel compiles only at `a_block_size = 512`; the 32-row pin is a HARD invariant of the 256-entry cache layout.

**Numerics — pure reassociation, FROZEN 1e-3 unchanged.** Caching only HOISTS the SAME products; the cache stores `dsc=d·f32_from_u32(sc)` / `dmm=dmin·f32_from_u32(mm)` from the byte-identical `get_scale_min_k4` on the SAME superblock bytes, and the per-nibble dequant is the SAME f32 expression (SAME multiply-then-subtract order, M3.5b line 181) with dsc/dmm read from shared instead of recomputed. The accumulation order (single-level K-loop, same coopmat tile order) is UNCHANGED. So the kernel is **BEHAVIORALLY identical** to M3.5b: the combined condition-aware metric (`|gpu−ref|/max(|ref|,Σ|wₖxₖ|) ≤` FROZEN **1e-3**) stays satisfied at K=256/512/14336, and a NEW cross-kernel **bit-identity** test (AT-1803, `max|y_cached − y_baseline| == 0`) asserts the M3.6 and M3.5b GPU outputs match bit-for-bit — a tighter anchor than oracle agreement, and the direct detector of the r1 silent-zeros class. The 1e-3 NEVER loosens.

**Correctness anchor — the real-GPU gate is mandatory; `spirv-val` is NOT the safety net.** AT-1803 bit-identity and the combined-metric K=256/512/14336 gates are `#[ignore]`/`AXC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS`-gated (typed-skip on Lavapipe), so CI ALONE would go GREEN on a miscompile (the r1 bug would have passed `spirv-val` + CI). The **ORCHESTRATOR MUST run AT-1800/1801/1802 + AT-1803 on REAL NVIDIA hardware before merge** (the project's mandatory real-GPU gate, anti-pattern #9). The silent-zeros failure mode WOULD be caught by the combined-metric K=14336 test (zeros → combined ≈ 1 ≫ 1e-3) AND by AT-1803 bit-identity (cached drops earlier-K → non-zero diff). CI-green (`spirv-val` + the no-GPU AT-1806) is NECESSARY but NOT a correctness proof.

**Acceptance (HONEST, falsifiable, measured on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000).** PRIMARY exit gate: cached kernel at **768³ ≥ 1.5× the M3.5b fused 4.81 TFLOPS (≥ 7.2 TFLOPS)** AND combined ≤ 1e-3 at K=256/512/14336. Secondary (reported): cached A/B-shape TFLOPS materially above M3.5b's 10.91, narrowing the 9.3× gap to llama's 101.88 (NOT a match promise — this is gap-NARROWING). **HONEST-NEGATIVE clause (strengthened for r3):** r3 trades 15/16 of the dequant-scale ALU recompute for **one extra unconditional `workgroup_barrier()` per k_block** (M3.5b had 2 per-k_block barriers, r3 has 3). Whether that nets positive is EXACTLY what the measurement decides — the milestone is gap-NARROWING, NOT guaranteed. If the cached kernel does NOT reach 1.5× at 768³ — because it is bandwidth-bound (q u8 reads) not ALU-bound, OR because the extra per-k_block barrier's cost EXCEEDS the saved dequant ALU, OR because +2 KB shared DROPS occupancy and net-regresses (the M3.3d failure mode) — that is REPORTED AS SUCH with measured numbers + diagnosis, NOT hidden and NOT papered over by loosening the gate.

**Reused infrastructure.** The f32-accumulator oracle (`common_q4km_f32ref.rs`), the resident bench methodology (`resident_q4km_matmul_rb_f32acc.rs`), and the A/B harness (`scripts/m34_llamacpp_ab.sh`) are REUSED; M3.6 adds a cached-kernel measurement path (`AXC_Q4KM_AB_F32ACC_CACHED`, `--fused-f32acc-cached`, `ab_results_fused_f32acc_cached.json`) and the AT-18xx test family.

**MEASURED RESULT (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, merged).** Dequant scale-caching is the campaign's largest single-step throughput win. The exit gate (768³ ≥ 1.5× M3.5b's 4.81 TFLOPS) was crushed at **2.88×** (13.84 TFLOPS); the dequant-bottleneck hypothesis is **confirmed** (not the provisioned honest-negative). Same-kernel speedup at every size, all **BIT-IDENTICAL to M3.5b** (AT-1803 PASS, n_diff=0) so correctness cost is ZERO (combined ≤ frozen 1e-3 inherited exactly): 256³ 0.59→1.49, 512³ 2.74→6.57, 768³ 4.81→13.84, 1024³ 7.93→23.28, **A/B(4096×512×14336) 10.91→42.86 (3.93×)**. The SAME-SHAPE A/B vs llama.cpp **102.49 TFLOPS** → **AXIOM 42.86 = llama 2.39× faster (ratio 0.418), numerically VALID** — collapsing the M3.5b gap from **9.3× to 2.39×** behind hand-tuned llama.cpp, with NO new language/codegen feature. Kill-criterion still FAIL (within-15% does not fire) but the gap is now in striking distance. Next: tile_k>16 sub-K-loop / double-buffered staging on NVIDIA, or the AMD/Intel half (llama.cpp known-weak; EB.1, hardware-blocked here).

*(Changelog: M3.6 (2026-06-06, MERGED r3) — Q4_K_M dequant scale-caching: new `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached.axc` (two `shared[f32,256]` scale caches; **FLAT single K-loop VERBATIM from M3.5b** with a workgroup-UNIFORM `if (k_block%16==0)` cooperative cache-FILL hoisting the dequant header + get_scale_min_k4 out of the per-nibble path; B/barriers/coopmat/single-level OpPhi/@strategy VERBATIM from M3.5b; a_block_size PINNED at 512) — a PURE SOURCE rewrite using the EXISTING shared[T,N] feature, **NO new builtin/codegen/capability/typecheck change, NO nested coopmat carry**. **r2→r3 FIX (Coder escalation, hard rule #5):** r2 placed the RAW barrier INSIDE the uniform `if (k_block%16==0)` fill, but the existing M3.2/OQ2 typecheck guard `TypecheckError::BarrierInDivergentContext` (typecheck.rs:2465) blanket-rejects ANY barrier inside ANY if-body (NO uniformity carve-out) → r2 does not compile. r3 adopts OPT-B: HOIST the RAW barrier OUT of the if to an UNCONDITIONAL once-per-k_block `workgroup_barrier()` after the fill and before A-staging (fill body stays conditional, writes only shared, no barrier inside → typecheck passes; the unconditional barrier still orders fill-WRITE → cache-READ and is strictly non-divergent). Cost: ONE extra unconditional barrier per k_block (2→3), trading 15/16 of the dequant ALU recompute for that barrier — net is what the bench decides (HONEST-NEGATIVE if it regresses). OPT-A (uniformity-aware carve-out to the guard) is OUT OF SCOPE for M3.6 (a possible future language improvement). STILL pure source, no codegen/typecheck change. **r1→r2 FIX:** r1's nested superblock-outer/k_block-inner restructure was a SILENT MISCOMPILE — `detect_loop_carried_coopmat` (body.rs:967, `descend_nested_loops=false`) never sees the inner-loop accumulator reassign → no outer OpPhi → accumulator resets to zero per superblock (only the last 256 of K survive); `spirv-val` passes it (valid-but-wrong). r2 keeps the flat single-level loop (proven single-level OpPhi carry) and gates the fill on a uniform predicate; the fill writes ONLY shared arrays so it does NOT trip the AT-1708 conditional-coopmat-reassign guard. GPU bit-within-tol K=256/512/14336 (AT-1800/1801/1802) + cross-kernel BIT-IDENTITY vs M3.5b (AT-1803) + cached honest multi-size TFLOPS with the 1.5×@768³ exit gate (AT-1804) + cached SAME-SHAPE A/B line (AT-1805) + CI compile/spirv-val/no-new-capability/metadata-shape/a_block_size-pin anchor (AT-1806). The GPU-gated AT-1803 + combined-K=14336 are the TRUE correctness gate (ORCHESTRATOR runs on real NVIDIA; spirv-val/CI alone are NOT sufficient). REUSES common_q4km_f32ref.rs + the resident bench + the A/B harness (--fused-f32acc-cached, ab_results_fused_f32acc_cached.json). FROZEN 1e-3 + accumulation order unchanged. AT-1800..AT-1806.)*


## 3.1.23 M3.7 — matmul-core throughput: double-buffered shared staging (software pipelining)

M3.6 (§3.1.22) closed the fused Q4_K_M f32-accumulator coopmat matmul gap to llama.cpp from **9.3× to 2.39×** by scale-caching the dequant front-end (**42.86 TFLOPS** at the A/B shape 4096×512×14336 vs llama's 102.49; 13.84 at 768³). The dequant is **no longer the bottleneck** — the residual gap is **matmul-CORE efficiency**. The M3.6 kernel stages SYNCHRONOUSLY: each k_block (a) stages A/B from global into ONE shared tile set, (b) `workgroup_barrier()`, (c) coopmat-loads from shared + 4 `coopmat_mul_add`, (d) WAR barrier. The tensor cores **STALL** during the next tile's global loads — global-load latency is fully **EXPOSED**. The classic GEMM fix is **double-buffering (software pipelining)**: stage k_block N+1's tiles into a SECOND shared buffer while the tensor cores compute on k_block N's already-staged buffer, overlapping memory latency with compute.

**Mechanism — PURE SOURCE REWRITE (prefetch-next, single FLAT K-loop, parity-indexed shared; NO compiler change).** Replace the single-buffer `shared a_tile: shared[f16, 512]` / `b_tile: shared[f16, 512]` with DOUBLE-buffered `shared[f16, 1024]` (= 2×512) tiles. Buffer p (p∈{0,1}) occupies the half-open slab `[p*512, p*512+512)`; the parity `parity = k_block - (k_block/2)*2` (= k_block % 2, anti-pattern #8 idiom, EXACTLY M3.6's `% 16`) folds into the coopmat_load **element_offset** — which the typechecker already accepts as ANY runtime `u32` expression (crates/axc-hir/src/typecheck.rs:2777), and which M3.6 already exercises with runtime shared indices. A **PROLOGUE** before the loop (top-level statements, `conditional_depth==0`) does, in order: fill the superblock-0 scale cache → **barrier (PROLOGUE-B1)** → stage buffer 0's A/B tiles → **barrier (PROLOGUE-B2)**. **TWO barriers, not one:** the cooperative cache fill is CROSS-LANE (lane L writes `cache_i=fi*32+L`; A-staging by lane L reads `cache_idx=a_row*8+is`, entries owned by OTHER lanes), so the fill→A-staging RAW is a real race; PROLOGUE-B1 separates the fill-WRITE from the staging-READ, PROLOGUE-B2 makes buffer 0 visible before the loop's first coopmat-load (mirroring M3.6's hoisted RAW barrier line 203 + post-staging barrier line 256).
The **single FLAT K-loop** body has ONE authoritative order (all blocks at the loop-body top level, `conditional_depth==0`, NONE inside any if): **(1)** cache FILL block `if (k_block+1)%16==0` (writes ONLY the shared scale caches; the `if` wraps no barrier); **(2)** **B1** — unconditional top-level `workgroup_barrier()` (covers the cache fill→read RAW AND the buffer WAR: B1 of iteration k+1 orders iteration k's coopmat-load read of `buf[k]` BEFORE iteration k+1's STAGE overwrites the same slab `buf[k]` — the buffer consumed in the IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING iteration); **(3)** STAGE/prefetch block — writes `buf[nxt_off+ei]` from global, reads the scale cache, with `if k_block+1 < num_k_blocks` guarding ONLY the global-read index/address expressions (NOT a barrier, NOT the accumulator); **(4)** **B2** — unconditional top-level `workgroup_barrier()` (covers STAGE write→next-iter coopmat-load RAW, and is load-bearing for the cross-superblock cache WAR); **(5)** coopmat-load `buf[cur_off]` + 4 `coopmat_mul_add` with the 4 f32 accumulators reassigned **UNCONDITIONALLY at the top level** — the EXISTING **single-level OpPhi N-accumulator carry** (M3.3c, AT-1731/1733) PRESERVED VERBATIM (NEVER inside any if, incl. the `kn<num_k_blocks` guard; NEVER in a nested loop). The **EPILOGUE is empty** — the last buffer is **COMPUTED** (block 5) on the final iteration; the `kn<num_k_blocks` guard suppresses ONLY that iteration's prefetch, never the compute → no under-compute, no over-read. Degenerate `num_k_blocks==1` (K=16): prologue stages buffer 0, the single iteration computes it, the guard skips the prefetch. `coopmat_store` is identical to M3.6.

**Why pure source (no builtin / no codegen / no capability / no typecheck change).** (1) Runtime coopmat_load offset: typecheck.rs:2777. (2) `shared[f16,1024]` within `MAX_SHARED_ELEMS=65536` / `MAX_SHARED_BYTES=65536` (crates/axc-hir/src/shared.rs:17,28). (3) Prologue/steady-state/epilogue barriers are all UNCONDITIONAL at `conditional_depth==0` → they PASS the `BarrierInDivergentContext` guard (typecheck.rs:2465); for-range bodies do NOT increment `conditional_depth` (only if/else does, typecheck.rs:1338-1355). (4) Single-level OpPhi carry preserved — accumulators reassigned unconditionally at the top level of ONE flat loop (`detect_loop_carried_coopmat`, crates/axc-codegen/src/body.rs:937-996, `descend_nested_loops=false` at :967). The staging-of-next loops are nested for-loops that touch ONLY the shared tiles, never the coopmat bindings.

**The M3.6-class trap, explicitly avoided.** The restructure does NOT introduce a nested loop carrying the accumulator (which silent-miscompiles via the `descend_nested_loops=false` path — the M3.6 r1 defect), does NOT conditionally reassign any coopmat binding (which trips the AT-1708 `LoopCarriedCoopMatConditionalReassignUnsupported` guard, body.rs:973), and does NOT place a barrier inside any if (which fails `BarrierInDivergentContext`). All barriers are hoisted to the loop body's top level / the prologue, exactly as M3.6 r3 hoisted its RAW barrier.

**Barriers — TARGET 2 unconditional per steady-state k_block (down from M3.6's 3), both top-level (`conditional_depth==0`).** **(B1)** post-fill / pre-stage barrier: orders (a) the in-iteration scale-cache fill-WRITE before the A-prefetch cache-READ, AND (b) the BUFFER WAR — B1 of iteration k+1 orders iteration k's coopmat-load (read of `buf[k]`) before iteration k+1's STAGE overwrites `buf[k]`. By ping-pong, iteration k+1's STAGE writes `nxt=(k+2)%2=k%2`, i.e. the SAME slab `buf[k]` the **immediately preceding** iteration's coopmat-load read (NOT "two iterations ago"); B1 sits between them, so the WAR is covered. The Coder MUST NOT relocate B1 after the STAGE. **(B2)** post-prefetch / pre-coopmat-load barrier: ensures (i) the CURRENT buffer's staging (issued as a prefetch the previous iteration) is visible before this iteration's load, AND (ii) is load-bearing for the cross-superblock cache WAR (superblock S's last cache-READ in iteration 16S+14 completes — past **B2 of that preceding iteration** — before sb S+1's fill overwrites the single-buffered cache in iteration 16S+15). A barrier REDUCTION (3→2) is a SECOND source of expected speedup ON TOP of the latency overlap. **2 barriers is a TARGET to VERIFY, not an assumption:** the ordering MUST be verified race-free by the cross-kernel bit-identity test (AT-1903) on real NVIDIA; if 2 prove insufficient (any non-zero diff), the 3rd is added back (matching M3.6) and double-buffering then competes purely on latency overlap with no barrier saving — reported honestly. spirv-val CANNOT catch a missing-barrier race.

**Scale-cache WAR (the one subtlety).** The scale caches rotate per-SUPERBLOCK (every 16 k_blocks), NOT per-k_block, so they stay SINGLE-buffered (`shared[f32,256]`, Option-1). The in-iteration fill (when `kn%16==0`) writes the WHOLE cache for superblock `kn/16` BEFORE any A-element of `kn` is staged, separated from the staging-read by an unconditional barrier; the previous superblock's last A-staging completed an earlier iteration (past the steady-state barrier). Option-2 fallback (double-buffer the caches → `shared[f32,512]`, +2 KB, no WAR) is adopted ONLY if Option-1's barrier accounting regresses, and ONLY after escalation (not a silent switch).

**Numerics — pure scheduling, FROZEN 1e-3 unchanged.** Double-buffering changes ONLY WHEN tiles are staged, not the arithmetic, the coopmat tile order, or the accumulation order. The kernel is therefore **BEHAVIORALLY identical** to M3.6: the cross-kernel **BIT-IDENTITY** anchor (AT-1903, `max|y_db − y_cached| == 0` at K=256/512/14336) is the load-bearing correctness gate AND the direct missing-barrier-race / parity-slip detector — STRONGER than oracle agreement. Backstopped by the COMBINED condition-aware metric `|gpu−ref|/max(|ref|, Σ|wₖxₖ|) ≤` FROZEN **1e-3** (AT-1900/1901/1902) vs `common_q4km_f32ref.rs`. The 1e-3 NEVER loosens.

**Acceptance (HONEST, falsifiable, measured on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000).** PRIMARY exit gate: the `_db` kernel at the A/B shape (4096×512×14336) **≥ 1.15× M3.6's 42.86 TFLOPS (≥ 49.3 TFLOPS)** AND combined ≤ 1e-3 at K=256/512/14336. Secondary (reported): 768³ vs M3.6's 13.84; whether the gap to llama's ~102.49 narrows further. **HONEST-NEGATIVE clause:** double-buffering DOUBLES the shared tile footprint (2 KB → 4 KB tiles, 6 KB total with caches). If that DROPS resident-workgroup occupancy and net-regresses (the M3.3d precedent — more shared/barriers → fewer resident WGs → slower), OR the kernel is already **COMPUTE-bound** at these sizes (double-buffering buys nothing on a non-memory-latency-bound GEMM), OR the 2-barrier scheme proves race-prone and needs the 3rd barrier back (erasing the barrier saving), the result is REPORTED AS A MEASURED NEGATIVE with `shared_memory_bytes` + occupancy diagnosis — NOT hidden, NOT papered over by loosening the gate. **Whether the GEMM is memory-latency-bound vs compute-bound at 768³ / A-B is THE open empirical question (OQ-1); the measurement decides.**

**Reused infrastructure.** `common_q4km_f32ref.rs` (oracle), the resident bench methodology (`resident_q4km_matmul_rb_f32acc_cached.rs` cloned → `resident_q4km_matmul_rb_f32acc_db.rs`), `scripts/m34_llamacpp_ab.sh` (`--fused-f32acc-db` sibling flag, `ab_results_fused_f32acc_db.json`), `compile_shared_examples.rs` (AT-1906 anchor), and the dispatch + bit-identity equiv scaffolding (cloned). New kernel: `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_db.axc` (headline); optional `examples/matmul_rb_coopmat_db.axc` (plain matmul, isolates the matmul-core effect, AT-1907).

**MEASURED RESULT (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, merged as documented HONEST-NEGATIVE).** Double-buffering REGRESSES this kernel ~3%. The double-buffered kernel is **BIT-IDENTICAL** to M3.6 (AT-1903 n_diff=0 at K=256/512/14336 + K=16 degenerate — the 2-barrier ping-pong is provably race-free, confirmed by both a formal emitted-SPIR-V barrier trace and runtime bit-identity), but SLOWER at every size: **A/B (m=4096,n=512,k=14336) 41.64 TFLOPS vs M3.6's 42.86 → 0.97× (exit gate ≥1.15×/≥49.3 MISSED)**; 768³ 13.0 vs 13.84 (0.94×); 256/512/1024 likewise. **The kernel is NOT global-load-latency-bound** — there was no latency to hide, and the doubled shared footprint (6144 vs 4096 bytes/workgroup) cut SM occupancy, a net loss (same mechanism as M3.3d's multi-subgroup negative). **M3.6 cached (42.86 TFLOPS, 2.39× behind llama 102.49) REMAINS THE PRODUCTION LEADER.** Merged anyway as a documented experiment (NOT the leader) because: (1) it proves AXIOM expresses software-pipelined double-buffering CORRECTLY from one annotated source (expressiveness result, pure-source, no new codegen); (2) the negative RULES OUT latency-hiding and points the next optimization at occupancy/compute (smaller shared footprint / larger register tiles / async-copy that doesn't double shared), not staging latency; (3) honest-negatives are recorded not hidden (M3.3d precedent). No spin: the gate was MISSED, the kernel is slower, reported as-is.

*(Changelog: M3.7 (MERGED, HONEST-NEGATIVE, r2 — spec-precision revision after dual review; mechanism UNCHANGED, prose made unambiguous: rewrote the steady-state to ONE correct B1/B2 loop-body order (the r1 single-barrier prose left the buffer WAR uncovered = silent race), changed the prologue from 1 to 2 barriers (cross-lane fill→stage RAW), added coder_directives + corrected the garbled B1 "two iterations ago" justification) — double-buffered shared staging: new `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_db.axc` (parity-indexed `shared[f16,1024]` A/B tiles; PROLOGUE fill→barrier→stage buffer 0→barrier (TWO barriers); single FLAT K-loop body order = FILL→B1→STAGE→B2→coopmat-load+mul_add, computing `buffer[k%2]` while PREFETCHING `buffer[(k+1)%2]`; 2 unconditional barriers/iter target, down from M3.6's 3; 4 f32 accumulators reassigned UNCONDITIONALLY at top level → single-level OpPhi carry VERBATIM M3.6; empty epilogue; `a_block_size_db`/`b_block_size_db` PINNED at 1024) — a PURE SOURCE rewrite using the EXISTING features (runtime coopmat_load offset typecheck.rs:2777; larger shared[f16] within MAX_SHARED_ELEMS shared.rs:17; unconditional barriers at conditional_depth==0 pass BarrierInDivergentContext typecheck.rs:2465; single-level OpPhi carry body.rs:937-996), **NO new builtin/codegen/capability/typecheck change**. M3.6-class trap explicitly avoided (no nested coopmat carry, no conditional coopmat reassign, no barrier-in-if). Scale-cache stays single-buffered (Option-1, in-iteration fill+barrier WAR; Option-2 double-buffered-cache fallback only on escalation). CORRECTNESS = cross-kernel BIT-IDENTITY vs M3.6 (AT-1903, max|y_db−y_cached|==0 at K=256/512/14336 — pure scheduling → bit-identical; the load-bearing missing-barrier-race detector) + combined condition-aware metric ≤ FROZEN 1e-3 (AT-1900/1901/1902). Honest multi-size TFLOPS + the ≥1.15×@A-B exit gate (AT-1904) + SAME-SHAPE A/B line (AT-1905, `--fused-f32acc-db`) + CI compile/spirv-val/no-new-capability/doubled-shared-bytes(6144)/metadata-shape/size-pin anchor (AT-1906) + optional plain-matmul `_db` bit-exact vs M3.3c (AT-1907). HONEST-NEGATIVE provisioned: doubled shared → occupancy regression (M3.3d precedent), or compute-bound GEMM (no latency to hide), or 2-barrier race forcing the 3rd back — any nets-slower outcome is REPORTED with measured numbers + diagnosis, NOT hidden. REUSES common_q4km_f32ref.rs + resident bench + A/B harness. FROZEN 1e-3 + accumulation order unchanged. AT-1900..AT-1907.)*

## 3.1.24 M3.8 — matmul-core occupancy/compute optimization (larger register tiles) [MERGED — HONEST-NEGATIVE — r2]

**MEASURED RESULT (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, merged as documented HONEST-NEGATIVE).** Larger register tiles REGRESS, the more so the larger. Both variants are BIT-IDENTICAL to M3.6 (AT-2003 n_diff=0 at K=256/512/14336, grid-independent) and combined ≤ frozen 1e-3 VALID, but much SLOWER: A/B (m=4096,n=512,k=14336) **4×4 = 28.19 TFLOPS (0.66×), 4×2 = 26.31 (0.61×)** vs M3.6's 42.86 (gate ≥1.15×/≥49.3 MISSED); 768³ 7.8/7.9 vs 13.84. **16 f32 accumulators is far too much register pressure → drastically fewer resident warps → net loss despite 2× arithmetic intensity** (regression LARGER than M3.7's 0.97×, confirming the mechanism). **COMBINED M3.7+M3.8 FINDING: the M3.6 2×2 kernel is at the OCCUPANCY/COMPUTE SWEET SPOT for this coopmat approach on NVIDIA** — both standard GEMM levers (latency-hiding M3.7, arithmetic-intensity M3.8) regress because the kernel is occupancy-bound; the remaining 2.39× to llama is NOT reachable by standard tile-tuning (would need a different coopmat shape / wave-level scheduling / driver codegen we don't control). **M3.6 cached REMAINS the production leader.** Merged as a documented experiment (NOT leader): proves AXIOM expresses 8/16-accumulator register blocking correctly (N-coopmat-phi at N=16, bit-identical) + establishes the occupancy ceiling. No spin: both variants MISSED the gate, slower, reported as-is.

M3.7 (§3.1.23) proved the fused Q4_K_M f32-accumulator coopmat matmul is **NOT global-load-latency-bound** — double-buffered staging REGRESSED ~3% (41.64 vs M3.6's 42.86 TFLOPS at the A/B shape), with the doubled shared footprint cutting SM occupancy. That negative **rules out latency-hiding and redirects the next optimization at OCCUPANCY/COMPUTE (arithmetic intensity), NOT staging latency**. M3.6 (§3.1.22, `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached.axc`, **42.86 TFLOPS, 2.39× behind llama's 102.49**) REMAINS the production leader; the residual gap is **matmul-CORE efficiency**.

**Hypothesis.** The M3.6 leader uses **2×2 register blocking** (4 loop-carried coopmat accumulators acc_00/01/10/11 → a 32×32 output tile, each staged A/B coopmat reused across 2 `coopmat_mul_add` → arithmetic intensity AI = 4 mul_adds / 4 shared loads = 1.0). **Raising the register-tile size increases AI**: each staged A/B tile — and each cached dequant scale, each address computation — feeds MORE `coopmat_mul_add`s, amortizing the dequant + staging + address-arithmetic front-end over more compute. M3.3c/M3.3d already proved (AT-1733) that **N coopmat phis in ONE flat K-loop emit with NO codegen change** (`detect_loop_carried_coopmat`, body.rs:937-996, iterates all coopmat bindings with no count cap and allocates one phi each; `emit_for_range` body.rs:1031+ loops over the carried set N-agnostically; `descend_nested_loops=false` body.rs:967 preserved; `axc-runtime/src/coopmat.rs` has no per-kernel accumulator limit), so larger register blocking is expressible **pure-source**.

**Mechanism — PURE SOURCE REWRITE (two new variant kernels; NO compiler change).** Two blocking factors are designed and **BOTH benched** (the bench picks the winner or reports all-negative honestly):

| Variant | Accums | Output tile | A/B coopmat loads | mul_adds | AI | Shared bytes/WG | A/B grid (m=4096,n=512) | WGs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M3.6 2×2 (leader) | 4 | 32×32 | 2A+2B | 4 | 1.0 | 4096 | (16,128,1) | 2048 |
| **4×2** | 8 | 64×32 | 4A+2B | 8 | 1.33 | 7168 | (16,64,1) | 1024 |
| **4×4** | 16 | 64×64 | 4A+4B | 16 | 2.0 | 8192 | (8,64,1) | 512 |

Each variant keeps M3.6 VERBATIM except: (a) N named f32 accumulators (8 or 16) all reassigned UNCONDITIONALLY at the flat-K-loop top level (single-level OpPhi N-carry); (b) the A staging extended to 64 rows (`a_block_size` PINNED 1024) and B to 32/64 cols; (c) the scale caches RESIZED to `shared[f32, 512]` each (64 A-rows × 8 sub-blocks; `cache_idx = a_row*8 + is`, a_row 0..63 → 0..511 exact; fill 16 fills/lane); (d) N additional `coopmat_load` of A row-tiles (offsets 0/256/512/768 stride 16) and B col-tiles (offsets 0/16[/32/48] stride 32/64). The conditional scale-cache fill + 3 unconditional top-level barriers + the multiply-then-subtract dequant order are VERBATIM M3.6.

**SHARED GROWTH is the GOOD kind** — it scales with WORK per workgroup (a bigger output tile genuinely needs more staged A rows + B cols), UNLIKE M3.7's double-buffer which doubled shared for the SAME work; 8192 bytes still fits the 16384-byte portable `maxComputeSharedMemorySize` floor.

**THE RISK — register pressure → occupancy regression.** A `matrix[f32,16,16]` accumulator = 256 f32 across the 32-lane subgroup = **8 f32/lane**; 4×4 = 16 accumulators = **~128 f32/lane** of accumulator registers alone, before A/B fragments + scalars. On Blackwell's 64K-reg/SM file this can drop the resident-warp count below the latency-hiding threshold and net SLOWER despite 2× AI — the SAME mechanism that made M3.3d multi-subgroup and M3.7 double-buffer net-negative. 4×2 (8 accumulators, ~64 f32/lane) is the moderate middle ground. Grid stays saturated at the A/B shape (4×4 = 512 WGs > 188 SMs → register-limited, not grid-limited); small sizes (256³: 4×4 = 16 WGs) WILL underfill and are reported per-size honestly (M3.3c precedent).

**Pure-source decision: NO new feature.** Both variants use ONLY existing features — N coopmat phis in one flat K-loop (body.rs:937-996, N-agnostic), runtime shared indexing for the resized cache and larger tiles (`shared[T,N]` ≤ 65536 elems, shared.rs:17/28; runtime `coopmat_load` offset typecheck.rs:2777), and the M3.6 conditional fill + 3 unconditional barriers at `conditional_depth==0` (BarrierInDivergentContext typecheck.rs:2465). **NO new builtin/codegen/capability/typecheck change.** M3.6-class traps explicitly avoided (no nested coopmat carry, no conditional coopmat reassign, no barrier-in-if).

**Correctness — bit-identical to M3.6 per output element.** Larger register blocking is the SAME arithmetic: each output 16×16 tile accumulates the SAME K-depth dot in the SAME single-level flat-K-loop order with the SAME per-tile coopmat decomposition and the SAME dequant expression — larger blocking adds MORE output tiles per workgroup, it does NOT regroup accumulation WITHIN any tile. So each output element is **BIT-IDENTICAL** to M3.6 → the cross-kernel bit-identity anchor (AT-2003, `max|y_bigrb − y_cached|.to_bits()==0` at K=256/512/14336) is the **EXPECTED first-line gate** and the load-bearing structural-deviation detector (wrong cache-resize index / wrong A-row or B-col coopmat offset/stride / accumulation-order change). The COMBINED condition-aware metric ≤ FROZEN **1e-3** vs `common_q4km_f32ref.rs` (AT-2000/2001/2002) is the **FLOOR AUTHORITY**. **NR-1 correctness-anchor fallback (r2):** if the driver spuriously reassociates HMMA across the larger register tile and AT-2003 shows a byte-diff DESPITE combined ≤ 1e-3, the milestone is **STILL CORRECT** — the combined floor is the authority a numerically-correct kernel cannot fail, and the non-zero AT-2003 diff is **documented + investigated as a driver-reassociation artifact** (analogous to M3.5b's catastrophic-cancellation finding), NOT auto-failed as a kernel bug. The 1e-3 NEVER loosens. (This prevents a spurious byte-diff across two *different* kernels — the driver's legal HMMA reassociation — from failing a numerically-correct milestone.)

**Hardening directives (r2, from dual design review — mechanism UNCHANGED).** **NR-2 per-variant grid/partial-tile guard:** the kernels do UNCONDITIONAL loads assuming exact tiling, so the bench/test grid pre-check MUST enforce a PER-VARIANT divisibility predicate before dispatch — **4×2 requires M%64==0 && N%32==0; 4×4 requires M%64==0 && N%64==0** — and SKIP-WITH-LOG (not OOB) any non-conforming size (do NOT inherit the cloned harness's `%32`-only guard). All current sizes (256/512/768/1024 cube + A/B 4096×512) divide evenly, so this is forward-protection against a future-added size. **NR-3 fail-fast 16-accumulator directive:** the FIRST implementation step is to write + compile + spirv-val + prepare-pipeline the 4×4 (16-accumulator) kernel and confirm **OpPhi count == 16** in one loop header BEFORE the test suite or the 4×2 variant; the codegen is proven N-agnostic so this SHOULD pass, the only unproven layer being the driver's register allocation for 16 live accumulators (an occupancy/perf question — the provisioned honest-negative, NOT a validity question). If the 16-accumulator kernel fails to COMPILE/validate (a genuine codegen gap), **STOP and escalate to the Architect (hard rule #5)** rather than work around. **Mechanical Coder notes** (each caught by AT-2003, stated to prevent mis-derivation): A-row coopmat_load offset = `i*256` (NOT `i*16`), stride 16; B stride is 32 for 4×2 (offsets 0/16) vs 64 for 4×4 (offsets 0/16/32/48) — do not cross them; scale-cache index = `a_row*8 + is` with a_row 0..63 (resized cache, 16 fills/lane); tighten the cloned equiv assert from `%32` to `%64`.

**Acceptance (HONEST, falsifiable, measured NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000).** PRIMARY exit gate: the BEST variant's A/B-shape (4096×512×14336) TFLOPS **≥ 1.15× M3.6's 42.86 = ≥ 49.3 TFLOPS**, AND combined ≤ 1e-3 at K=256/512/14336, AND bit-identical to M3.6 (AT-2003 n_diff==0). **HONEST-NEGATIVE clause:** more accumulators = more registers = possible occupancy regression (M3.7/M3.3d precedent) that nets slower. If BOTH variants miss 49.3, the result is REPORTED AS A MEASURED NEGATIVE with per-variant TFLOPS + `shared_memory_bytes` + a register-pressure/occupancy diagnosis — NOT hidden, NOT papered over by loosening the gate. M3.6 (42.86) remains the leader unless a variant beats the gate.

**Reused infrastructure.** `common_q4km_f32ref.rs` (oracle), the resident bench methodology (`resident_q4km_matmul_rb_f32acc_cached.rs` cloned → `_bigrb.rs`), `scripts/m34_llamacpp_ab.sh` (`--bigrb` flag, `ab_results_fused_f32acc_bigrb_4x2/4x4.json`), `compile_shared_examples.rs` (AT-2006 anchor), and the dispatch + bit-identity equiv scaffolding (cloned). New kernels: `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached_4x2.axc` and `_4x4.axc`.

**Implementation status (Coder, M3.8).** Both kernels written PURE-SOURCE (no compiler change). **NR-3 fail-fast PASSED**: the 4×4 (16-accumulator) kernel compiles via `compile_source_with_assignments`, passes `spirv-val` (Vulkan 1.1), and emits **exactly 16 OpPhi in ONE loop-header basic block** (the single-level N-accumulator carry — no extra phis), `shared_memory_bytes == 8192`, 3 OpControlBarrier, capability set byte-identical to M3.6. The 4×2 variant: **8 OpPhi**, `shared_memory_bytes == 7168`, same capability set. AT-2006 (CI, no-GPU) asserts both. AT-2000/2001/2002 (combined ≤ 1e-3), AT-2003 (bit-identity + NR-1 combined report), AT-2004/2005 (bench + A/B lines, winner + ≥49.3 gate) are `#[ignore]`-gated for the orchestrator's NVIDIA run. NR-2 per-variant divisibility guard implemented in the bench + both dispatch tests (skip-with-log, never OOB). The script flags are `--fused-f32acc-rb44` / `--fused-f32acc-rb42`.*

*(Status: IMPLEMENTED — r2 after dual design review. Optimistic=APPROVE; Pessimistic=NEEDS_REVISION (core sound — N=16 emits, bit-identity holds, bounds safe, carry preserved — three fixable hardening items, now folded in: NR-1 correctness-anchor fallback, NR-2 per-variant grid guard, NR-3 fail-fast 16-accumulator). Mechanism UNCHANGED. AT-2000..AT-2006.)*

## 3.1.25 M4.1 — PyTorch frontend with CUDA↔Vulkan zero-copy interop [OPENS THE M4 ADOPTION PHASE] [Phase 1 MERGED — saxpy proven zero-copy]

**MEASURED RESULT (Phase 1 merged, NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 / 580.x driver / CUDA 12.8 / torch 2.12).** The CUDA↔Vulkan zero-copy path WORKS end-to-end. **The G-6 pre-gate passed: timeline-semaphore-over-OPAQUE_FD interop works on the 580.x driver** (a Vulkan timeline semaphore exported as an fd → `cudaImportExternalSemaphore` type 9 → round-trip signal/wait completes; the binary-semaphore-pair fallback is wired + live-exercised). **saxpy is BIT-EXACT zero-copy** (max abs diff 0.0): a torch CUDA tensor and the AXIOM Vulkan kernel share ONE dedicated physical allocation — AT-2101 proves same-memory BOTH directions (CUDA writes → Vulkan reads back == pattern; Vulkan writes → CUDA reads), with a hard 0/0 staging-buffer/copy assertion (genuinely staging-free, not a host bridge). The cross-API handshake (torch signals V1 → Vulkan waits V1/signals V2 → torch waits V2, one timeline semaphore, same CUDA stream, monotone (V1,V2)) is race-free over 1000 iterations + a 5000-dispatch loop, bit-exact. Lifetime is double-free/UAF-safe (single fd owner = Python; non-owning `__cuda_array_interface__` torch view; exact teardown drain→cudaDestroy→vkFree; idempotent close). Fail-closed UUID matching (no silent different-GPU / no host-copy fallback). **HONEST G-1 finding: the 580.x driver ACCEPTS a flags=0 dedicated-mismatch (does NOT fail-closed) — so the silent-aliasing class is real; the frontend ALWAYS matches `cudaExternalMemoryDedicated` and relies on the AT-2101 same-memory proof, never on driver rejection.** Built as `crates/axc-py` (PyO3, abi3 wheel via maturin) + `py/axiom_compute` (cuda-python REQUIRED, fail-closed if absent — no host-copy degradation). Pipeline: Architect r2 → Coder (spike-first) → QA (independently rebuilt the wheel + ran 12 GPU tests + 64MB×50 stress) → dual code review (pessimistic found + got fixed: a critical per-dispatch command-buffer leak → reset-and-reuse with a timeline-retirement UAF guard, a mid-flight-close UAF window → device_wait_idle hard-fallback, an fd-leak-on-import-failure, a same-stream warn-guard, a docstring overclaim) → both APPROVE. 13/13 Python GPU tests pass, 945 cargo tests, clippy clean. **DEFERRED to follow-up phases: the Q4_K_M headline op (AT-2103, needs the strategy-assignment compile path + GGUF weights wired through PyO3) + the honest latency report (AT-2105), torch.library op (M4.2), torch.compile backend (M4.3).** Honest perf framing: zero-copy ELIMINATES host transfer but the Vulkan kernel is ~34% of cuBLAS — NOT a torch.matmul replacement; the win is no-host-copy + real torch interop. One Phase-1 residual flagged for M4.2: no timeout on the CUDA data-path waits; NVIDIA-only EXCLUSIVE-mode sync. AT-2100..AT-2110.

M0–M3.8 built the COMPILER + RUNTIME: an annotated `.axc` source → SPIR-V → a fused Q4_K_M coopmat matmul running at **42.86 TFLOPS** on the NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (M3.6, §3.1.22, the production leader, 2.39× behind llama.cpp). **M4 is the ADOPTION phase: make those kernels CALLABLE from the framework the ecosystem actually uses — PyTorch.** M4.1 is the first M4 milestone and a NEW domain (Python/PyO3 + CUDA↔Vulkan external-memory interop), distinct from the kernel-codegen work of M3.x. The goal: a **`pip`-installable** PyTorch frontend where a torch **CUDA** tensor feeds an AXIOM-Compute **Vulkan** kernel on the **SAME physical GPU** with **ZERO host copies** — so a ≤10-line PyTorch program calls an AXIOM kernel (saxpy as the trivial proof; the M3.6 Q4_K_M matmul as the headline) as a real torch op, results correct, no host round-trip.

**Interop architecture (the core of M4.1).** *(1) Memory direction — Vulkan allocates + exports, CUDA imports — UNCONDITIONALLY DEDICATED, dedication bit MATCHED both sides (G-1, CRITICAL).* AXIOM allocates the shared buffer DEVICE_LOCAL + EXPORTABLE (`VkExternalMemoryBufferCreateInfo` on the buffer + `VkExportMemoryAllocateInfo` handleTypes=`OPAQUE_FD_BIT` on the allocation, on top of the existing `allocate_device_local_buffer` path, buffers.rs:254/311) and exports an OS fd with `vkGetMemoryFdKHR` (VK_KHR_external_memory_fd). **The exportable allocation is made UNCONDITIONALLY DEDICATED**: `VkMemoryDedicatedAllocateInfo{ buffer }` is chained into `VkMemoryAllocateInfo` (this is natural and free — `allocate_device_local_buffer` already does one `VkDeviceMemory` per buffer with a whole-allocation offset-0 bind, NOT a suballocating pool, so there is no suballocation-export bug). **CRITICAL — the dedication bit must MATCH on BOTH sides.** The fd does NOT carry the dedication bit; CUDA's `cudaExternalMemoryHandleDesc.flags` (driver_types.h:2588-2590) must be set to `cudaExternalMemoryDedicated` (0x1) to MATCH a Vulkan allocation made with `VkMemoryDedicatedAllocateInfo`. A mismatch causes EITHER import failure (fail-closed, acceptable) OR wrong-memory aliasing (SILENT corruption, **NOT** acceptable — the un-spirv-val-catchable class this milestone exists to avoid). Therefore the bit is PLUMBED Rust→Python out-of-band alongside the fd + size: `ExternalBuffer`/`SharedBufferSet` exposes a `dedicated: bool` accessor (always `true` for now), and `_cuda_interop.import_memory_fd` sets `memHandleDesc.flags = cudaExternalMemoryDedicated` to match. Python imports the fd into CUDA via `cudaImportExternalMemory` + `cudaExternalMemoryGetMappedBuffer` → a CUDA device pointer that torch wraps as a CUDA tensor. Vulkan-allocates (not torch-allocates→Vulkan-imports) is chosen because AXIOM already OWNS the allocation, memory-type selection, and binding plan; the reverse would force AXIOM to reconcile foreign memory with its descriptor machinery AND entangle with torch's caching allocator (sub-allocations that aren't individually exportable). The fd is the clean ABI boundary (and the dedication bit rides beside it). *(2) Synchronization — one TIMELINE external semaphore, NO host sync; same-stream + monotone-counter pinned (G-5/G-7).* Vulkan creates+exports a timeline semaphore (`VkExportSemaphoreCreateInfo` OPAQUE_FD + `vkGetSemaphoreFdKHR`); Python imports it (`cudaImportExternalSemaphore`, TimelineSemaphoreFd, type 9). The handshake (monotone V0<V1<V2): torch writes → `cudaSignalExternalSemaphoresAsync(V1)` → Vulkan compute submit WAITS V1 @COMPUTE_SHADER / SIGNALS V2 (`VkTimelineSemaphoreSubmitInfo`) → `cudaWaitExternalSemaphoresAsync(V2)` before any torch read. **Same-stream consistency (G-5):** ALL torch ops touching the shared tensors AND both `cudaSignal/WaitExternalSemaphoresAsync` calls MUST be on ONE CUDA stream `S` (`cudaWaitExternalSemaphoresAsync` is a STREAM-ORDERED enqueue, not a host block — a stream mismatch = a silent torn read). The Python API captures one stream (`torch.cuda.current_stream()`), passes its handle into every signal/wait, and DOCUMENTS that all shared-tensor ops must use that stream. **Monotone timeline counter (G-7):** the Kernel holds a single monotone `AtomicU64`; each `__call__` reserves a FRESH `(V1,V2)` pair (`V1 = fetch_add`, `V2 = V1+1`, the next call starts above `V2`) so concurrent/repeated calls NEVER reuse timeline values (a reused/decreasing value on a timeline does NOT race — it DEADLOCKS). AT-2104's 1000-iter loop advances the counter 1000× (1000 distinct triples), never reusing 3 values. NO `vkQueueWaitIdle`, NO `cudaDeviceSynchronize` on the data path — the only cross-API ordering is timeline monotonicity. This is the **#1 correctness risk** (a missing/mis-ordered wait = a cross-API torn read, the analogue of the M3.6/M3.7 barrier races, and **spirv-val cannot catch it**). *(3) Device matching — FAIL-CLOSED.* Enumerate physical devices, `get_physical_device_properties2` with `VkPhysicalDeviceIDProperties` chained, select the device whose `deviceUUID` byte-equals the CUDA device UUID (RAW 16 bytes from `cudaDeviceGetAttribute`/`cudaDeviceProp::uuid`, NOT the dashed `GPU-xxxx` string re-parsed; this box: GPU-e9ac657f-a144-30a8-5a09-2ed93eb0e93e); no match → `DispatchError::NoUuidMatchedDevice` → `ZeroCopyUnavailable` — NEVER a different GPU, NEVER a silent host-copy (an OPAQUE_FD import across GPUs fails anyway, so UUID-match is correctness). The selection overrides context.rs:340-357. *(4) Rust↔CUDA split — CUDA import on the PYTHON side; single fd owner (G-3).* The Rust crate does ONLY Vulkan EXPORT (links ash, already a dep; does NOT link libcudart); the CUDA IMPORT runs in Python via cuda-python (preferred) or ctypes→`libcudart.so.12`. This lands the import in the SAME CUDA context torch uses (avoiding a context-mismatch bug class a separate Rust cudart link would invite), keeps the Rust wheel CUDA-agnostic (simpler maturin/CI), and wrapping the imported ptr as a torch tensor is itself a torch operation. The lower-risk path. **Single fd owner (G-3, no double-close):** the OPAQUE_FD has EXACTLY ONE owner. Rust hands Python the fd via `into_raw_fd()` (RELEASING Rust's `OwnedFd` ownership — the raw `i32` is transferred), and the **Python side owns the close** after `cudaImportExternalMemory` consumes it. Rust does NOT also close the handed-off fd in `Drop`. (On Linux OpaqueFd, CUDA does not dup on import; the app closes it after import — so exactly one of {Rust, Python} closes, and it is Python.) AT-2106 asserts the fd is closed exactly once (fd-leak / sentinel check).

**The zero-copy dispatch path is NEW and STAGING-FREE (G-2, CRITICAL scoping).** `dispatch_zero_copy(bufs, workgroups, push_constants, wait_value=V1, signal_value=V2)` is a GENUINELY NEW submit path — it does NOT reuse `resident.rs`'s / `kernel_handle.rs`'s host-copy upload (staging→device) + readback (device→staging) path. The existing resident path (1) allocates its OWN non-exportable device-local buffers, (2) UPLOADS inputs through a transient host-copied staging buffer (resident.rs:431-527), and (3) READS BACK through a HOST_VISIBLE staging buffer with a host copy (resident.rs:986-1102) — reusing ANY of that would round-trip the data through host memory and the zero-copy claim collapses. The new path instead: **(a)** binds the caller's EXPORTABLE dedicated `ExternalBuffer`s DIRECTLY as the kernel's SSBOs (the input/output SSBOs ARE the exported buffers — no staging buffer allocated, no map, no memcpy); **(b)** records ONLY the compute submit with `VkTimelineSemaphoreSubmitInfo{ wait=[V1] @COMPUTE_SHADER, signal=[V2] }` against the EXTERNAL timeline (mirroring the existing working submit at kernel_handle.rs:1336-1371, but with the external semaphore and NO host fence on the data path); **(c)** records NO `vkCmdCopyBuffer` to/from staging. The descriptor pool/set for the external buffers is allocated by the new path itself (one descriptor set binding the N `ExternalBuffer`s, sized at `dispatch_zero_copy` setup, mirroring the existing descriptor-set allocation in the resident path but pointing at the exported buffers). **Guards:** AT-2101 proves same-physical-memory on the DIRECT-shared-allocation input path (the user allocates the input directly in the shared buffer — not the d2d-copy path), in BOTH directions (CUDA-write→Vulkan-read AND Vulkan-write→CUDA-read); and a "no staging buffer allocated on the zero-copy path" assertion guards the absence of a host bridge. (The OQ-4 d2d-copy convenience path — for an arbitrary pre-existing torch tensor — is "no HOST round-trip but NOT input-zero-copy" and is documented as such; the headline AT-2101 proof uses the TRUE zero-copy path.)

**The PyO3 package + teardown order (G-4).** New crate `crates/axc-py` (cdylib, PyO3 abi3-py312) built by maturin into the `axiom_compute` Python package (`py/`). `axiom_compute.compile_kernel(source) → Kernel`; `Kernel(*torch_cuda_tensors) → torch_cuda_tensor` which (a) validates tensors are CUDA on the matched device (else `ZeroCopyUnavailable` — no silent host-copy), (b) shares memory zero-copy via the imported CUDA pointers, (c) dispatches via the new staging-free path, (d) syncs via the timeline semaphore on the captured stream `S`, (e) returns a zero-copy view of the Vulkan-written output. **The output torch tensor is a NON-OWNING view** wrapped via `__cuda_array_interface__`/`from_blob` with a deleter tied to the `ImportedExternalMemory` handle (NOT torch's caching allocator) — so torch NEVER frees the foreign `devPtr`. **EXACT teardown order in `Kernel.close()` / the Rust `Drop` (G-4):** (1) DRAIN in-flight GPU work — on the no-host-fence production path, `close()` MUST wait the timeline semaphore to ≥ the last signaled `V2` (or `device_wait_idle`) BEFORE freeing, else `cudaDestroyExternalMemory` races a live kernel; (2) drop/release the torch tensor view (release the Python reference) so no later torch op aliases the ptr; (3) `cudaDestroyExternalMemory` (frees the CUDA import + invalidates `devPtr`); (4) `cudaDestroyExternalSemaphore`; (5) Rust `vkFreeMemory` + `vkDestroyBuffer` + `vkDestroySemaphore`. (If (5) `vkFreeMemory` ran before (3), CUDA would hold a dangling import; if (3) ran while the torch view still aliased the ptr, a later torch op is UAF — hence this exact order.) `close()` is IDEMPOTENT (an already-destroyed guard flag); AT-2106 calls it TWICE and asserts no second free / no double fd-close, under Vulkan validation layers. New runtime modules `external_memory.rs` (exportable dedicated buffer + fd export + exportable timeline semaphore) and `device_uuid.rs` (UUID match); context.rs gains an `external_interop` option enabling VK_KHR_external_memory_fd + VK_KHR_external_semaphore_fd device extensions, forcing `timelineSemaphore` ON (context.rs:509-511,536-540), and UUID-selecting the physical device. `cargo test --workspace` stays green via `default-members` excluding the cdylib axc-py; a SEPARATE self-hosted-NVIDIA CI job runs maturin + py/tests (the Lavapipe software-GPU CI has no CUDA / NVIDIA ICD).

**Phased plan (MULTI-SESSION, ~3000-5000 LOC, new domain).** **Phase 1 (THIS milestone)** proves the zero-copy path end-to-end on ONE op: **1a** Vulkan-side export plumbing (exportable buffer — **`VkMemoryDedicatedAllocateInfo` is a Phase-1a DEFAULT, unconditionally on, NOT "maybe"** — + fd + timeline semaphore) + UUID-matched context, with a Phase-1a micro-test that the chosen DEVICE_LOCAL type ACTUALLY imports via `cudaImportExternalMemory` (flags=`cudaExternalMemoryDedicated`) on this box BEFORE wiring saxpy (surfaces the dedicated-allocation requirement as the first, cheapest failure); **1b** CUDA import + the timeline handshake — the **VERY FIRST 1b deliverable is the timeline-over-OPAQUE_FD interop SPIKE** (a standalone gate, ordered before everything else in 1b): export a Vulkan timeline-semaphore fd → `cudaImportExternalSemaphore` type `cudaExternalSemaphoreHandleTypeTimelineSemaphoreFd` (=9) → a round-trip `cudaSignal(V1)` / Vulkan-waits-V1 / Vulkan-signals-V2 / `cudaWait(V2)`. The type-9 constant is confirmed present in driver_types.h:2766 with `.params.fence.value`, but the RUNTIME behavior on this box's **580.x NVIDIA driver** MUST be PROVEN before the rest of Phase 1 depends on it. A **binary-semaphore-pair FALLBACK** (two binary external semaphores, one per direction) is WIRED and is EXERCISED (forced, not dead code) if the spike fails — reported, NOT silently degraded. Then 1c; **1c** the PyO3 package + a saxpy zero-copy call returning a correct torch CUDA tensor, then the M3.6 Q4_K_M matmul as the headline. The minimal first end-to-end proof is **saxpy** (trivial, isolates the interop from kernel complexity). **DEFERRED:** M4.2 `torch.library.custom_op` registration; M4.3 a `torch.compile`/Inductor backend; M4.4 multi-op + AMD/Intel via the same path; Windows OPAQUE_WIN32 (M4.1 is Linux OPAQUE_FD only).

**Acceptance — HONEST + falsifiable, on the REAL NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (no mocking).** PRIMARY (correctness): (a) **ZERO-COPY VERIFIED** — the CUDA-imported pointer and the Vulkan allocation are the SAME physical memory, PROVEN by a write-through-one-API / visible-through-the-other readback identity (AT-2101), NOT assumed; (b) saxpy BIT-EXACT vs a torch reference on the same tensors (AT-2102); (c) Q4_K_M matmul within the **FROZEN combined condition-aware metric ≤ 1e-3** at K=256/512/14336 (AT-2103, reusing the M3.x `common_q4km_f32ref` oracle, cross-checked against torch); the 1e-3 NEVER loosens. PRIMARY (race-free): 1000+ -iteration determinism of the cross-API handshake (AT-2104). PRIMARY (no silent host-copy): fail-closed on CPU/wrong-device/missing-ext tensors (AT-2107). REPORTED (honest, NOT a pass/fail gate): end-to-end latency — AXIOM zero-copy **<** AXIOM host-copy baseline (the win: no host round-trip) AND **stated slower than native torch.matmul/cuBLAS on raw compute** (the Vulkan kernel is ~34% of cuBLAS; AT-2105). **We do NOT promise beating cuBLAS** — the value is real torch interop + zero host round-trip, reported with the cuBLAS comparison plainly, no spin (R-6). HYGIENE: imported-CUDA-handle lifetime / no double-free under Vulkan validation (AT-2106), CI green + maturin wheel builds (AT-2108).

**Top risks.** R-1 (CRITICAL) cross-API sync race — single timeline semaphore + the exact 5-step monotone handshake + same-stream consistency (G-5) + a monotone `AtomicU64` reserving a fresh `(V1,V2)` per call (G-7) + 1000-iter determinism proof (AT-2104). R-2 (HIGH) imported-ptr lifetime — torch must NOT free foreign memory via its caching allocator (non-owning `from_blob`/CAI view with a deleter tied to `ImportedExternalMemory`, NOT the caching allocator); `ImportedExternalMemory` owns + destroys the CUDA external handle (`cudaDestroyExternalMemory`); EXACT teardown order with in-flight drain (G-4); single fd owner = Python, close-once (G-3); idempotent `close()` (AT-2106). R-3 (HIGH) device-UUID match failure → fail-closed raw-16-byte compare (AT-2100). **R-4 (CRITICAL — elevated from MED, G-1) the dedicated-allocation flag MISMATCH trap** — the exportable allocation is UNCONDITIONALLY dedicated (`VkMemoryDedicatedAllocateInfo`, a Phase-1a default) AND the dedication bit is PLUMBED Rust→Python so `cudaExternalMemoryHandleDesc.flags = cudaExternalMemoryDedicated` MATCHES; a mismatch = import failure (fail-closed, OK) or silent wrong-memory aliasing (NOT OK); surface non-importable types as `ExternalMemoryUnsupported`, no host-fallback. R-5 (MED) maturin/CUDA/CI build complexity → default-members excludes axc-py, self-hosted NVIDIA CI job. R-6 (LOW-but-honest) perf vs cuBLAS — explicitly NOT gated on beating it.

**User prerequisite.** **maturin is NOT installed on this box** (VERIFIED: `which maturin` fails) — `pip install maturin` is a USER prereq for the BUILD (`cd py && maturin develop --release`), analogous to the llama.cpp build tooling in M3.4. Runtime additionally needs CUDA 12.x + torch cu12x + an NVIDIA Vulkan ICD with the external_*_fd extensions (all VERIFIED present on this box except maturin). The package fails-CLOSED with a clear prereq message if anything is missing — it does NOT silently degrade to host-copy.

**Phase-1 IMPLEMENTED result (Coder, 2026-06-08, real NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, 580.x driver).** The G-6 timeline-over-OPAQUE_FD interop **SPIKE PASSES on this driver** (AT-2110): a Vulkan timeline-semaphore fd → `cudaImportExternalSemaphore` type=9 → `cudaSignal(V1)`/Vulkan-waits-V1/Vulkan-signals-V2/`cudaWait(V2)` round-trip completes — so Phase 1 uses the TIMELINE path; the binary-semaphore-pair fallback is wired + exercised live. **Zero-copy is VERIFIED, not assumed** (AT-2101): on the direct-shared-allocation path, the Vulkan kernel reads CUDA-written input AND its write is visible to CUDA — same physical memory, both directions. **saxpy is BIT-EXACT** (AT-2102, max abs diff 0.0), with **0 staging buffers and 0 `vkCmdCopyBuffer` records** on the zero-copy path (AT-2109 G-2). The cross-API handshake is **race-free over 1000 iterations** (AT-2104), each a distinct monotone `(V1,V2)` triple (G-7). Fail-closed on a bogus UUID (AT-2107); `close()` is idempotent with no fd leak and a non-owning torch view (AT-2106 G-3/G-4). **HONEST G-1 finding:** on this 580.x driver, importing a DEDICATED export with `flags=0` (a MISMATCH) is **ACCEPTED** (the driver does NOT fail-closed) — i.e. the silent-aliasing class is real here, so the frontend ALWAYS sets `cudaExternalMemoryDedicated` to MATCH and never relies on the driver rejecting a mismatch; the matched import's correctness is the AT-2101 same-memory proof. Implemented: `crates/axc-runtime/{external_memory.rs,device_uuid.rs}` + context interop path + the NEW `dispatch_zero_copy`; `crates/axc-py` (PyO3) + `py/axiom_compute` (`compile_kernel`/`Kernel`/`SharedSession`, `_cuda_interop`). **Q4_K_M headline (AT-2103/2105) DEFERRED to a follow-up session** (the saxpy zero-copy proof + all G-items + the spike are complete). **[CLOSED by Phase 2 — §3.1.26: AT-2103 passes at K=256/512/14336 (combined ≤ 2.05e-6 ≪ frozen 1e-3); AT-2105 honest latency reported (zero-copy 1.53 ms < host-copy 2.95 ms, ~53% of f32-cuBLAS, no beat-cuBLAS claim).]** `cargo test` (default-members) green: 945 passed / 0 failed; 12 Python GPU tests passed.*

*(Status: PROPOSED — Architect (M4.1, **r2** after dual design review: optimistic APPROVE, pessimistic NEEDS_REVISION — the handshake has NO uncovered ordering hazard and a COMPATIBLE timeline-fd semaphore type, four fixable gaps pinned). Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/M4.1-architect.json`. r2 pins: **G-1** dedicated-allocation flag MATCHED both sides (unconditional `VkMemoryDedicatedAllocateInfo` + plumbed `dedicated` bit + `cudaExternalMemoryDedicated` on import); **G-2** `dispatch_zero_copy` is a NEW staging-free submit path binding the exportable buffers directly (no reuse of resident.rs host-copy upload/readback); **G-3** single fd owner = Python (Rust `into_raw_fd`, no Drop-close); **G-4** exact teardown order with in-flight drain + non-owning torch view; **G-5** same-stream consistency; **G-7** monotone `AtomicU64` per-call `(V1,V2)`; **G-6** timeline-over-fd interop SPIKE is the FIRST Phase-1b deliverable with a binary-semaphore-pair fallback exercised on failure. Architecture otherwise UNCHANGED. Phase 1 = UUID-matched Vulkan context + dedicated external-memory export + CUDA import + timeline external-semaphore sync + one zero-copy kernel call from Python returning a correct torch CUDA tensor (saxpy proven, Q4_K_M headline). torch.library + torch.compile DEFERRED to M4.2/M4.3. AT-2100..AT-2110 (r2 adds AT-2109 dedicated-flag-match + no-staging-buffer assertion, AT-2110 timeline-over-fd spike + binary-pair fallback). Ready for Coder Phase 1a.)*
