## 3.1.26 M4.1 Phase 2 — the M3.6 Q4_K_M matmul as the HEADLINE op through the zero-copy frontend [MERGED — closes AT-2103 + AT-2105]

Phase 1 (§3.1.25) proved the CUDA↔Vulkan zero-copy interop on **saxpy** (bit-exact, 0/0 staging, race-free handshake) but **DEFERRED** the real headline: the M3.6 Q4_K_M dequant+coopmat matmul (`examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached.axc`, 42.86 TFLOPS leader). Phase 2 runs THAT kernel through the same zero-copy path and closes **AT-2103** (Q4_K_M correctness within the FROZEN combined condition-aware 1e-3 at K=256/512/14336) + **AT-2105** (honest latency report). No new kernel, no new sync machinery — the M3.6 source and the Phase-1 handshake/teardown are reused VERBATIM.

**Three blockers Phase 1 named, and the Phase-2 design for each.**

**(1) Strategy-assignment compile through PyO3.** The M3.6 kernel carries `@strategy { rb_m: ?[2], rb_n: ?[2], tile_k: ?[16], a_block_size: ?[512], b_block_size: ?[512] }`; Phase-1's `compile_kernel` only calls `compile_source_with_meta` (no hole resolution) and would trip the `UnresolvedStrategyHole` backstop. FIX: `compile_kernel(source, device_uuid, strategy=None)` gains an OPTIONAL `strategy: dict` kwarg that, when present, routes to the EXISTING `axc_driver::compile_source_with_assignments` (the M2.3 strategy-value path the resident bench uses, `resident_q4km_matmul_rb_f32acc_cached.rs:355`) — source-text substitution of `?name`→value + `@strategy`-block strip, fully-resolved codegen. The Python `Q4KMMatmul` passes the leader assignment `{rb_m:2, rb_n:2, tile_k:16, a_block_size:512, b_block_size:512}`. The saxpy `strategy=None` path is unchanged. `AT-2103d` asserts `strategy=None` on the M3.6 source FAILS at compile (the kwarg is load-bearing).

**(2) Buffer plan — upload-once weights vs per-call zero-copy x/C.** The kernel binds THREE SSBOs: `[0]=q: readonly_buffer[u8]`, `[1]=x: readonly_buffer[f16]`, `[2]=C: buffer[f32]` (M,N,K,n_blocks_per_row are PUSH CONSTANTS). The split:
- **q (Q4_K_M weights) = UPLOAD-ONCE** exportable dedicated shared buffer. Written ONCE at `upload_weights()` (a device-side torch copy into the shared view on stream S), then REUSED across every `matmul()`. The static-weights carve-out (Phase 1: "static weights uploaded-once is fine + NOT a per-call host copy"). Byte layout = ggml Q4_K_M: M rows × n_blocks_per_row × 144-byte superblocks, row-major; within a superblock `[0..2]`=d (f16), `[2..4]`=dmin (f16), `[4..16]`=12 packed 6-bit (sc,m) scales, `[16..144]`=128 bytes of 4-bit quants — EXACTLY what `dequant_q4km_row_f32` reads (`common_q4km_f32ref.rs:79`) and the `.axc` fill/staging reads.
- **x (f16 activations) = ZERO-COPY** per-call torch CUDA tensor (`torch.float16`, row-major `x[k*N+col]`, size `2·K·N`).
- **C (f32 output) = ZERO-COPY** per-call torch CUDA tensor (row-major `y[m·N+col]`, size `4·M·N`, read back directly, NOT widened from f16).

All three are allocated in ONE `SharedSession` (`allocate_shared([q_size, x_size, c_size])`, the existing 3-buffer API). The **G-2 staging-free invariant holds for the PER-CALL path** (x-in/C-out): `staging_buffers_allocated()==0`, `copy_buffer_records()==0` across `matmul()` calls (`AT-2103c`). Headline A/B shape m=4096,n=512,k=14336 → q≈33 MB, x≈14.7 MB, C≈8.4 MB (well under the 50 GB cap). Push constants assembled generically via a new `CompiledKernel::scalar_layout()` (name/offset/size per scalar) — the Python analogue of the bench's `assemble_pc`.

**(3) The oracle (AT-2103) — REUSE the Rust f32-accumulator reference, do NOT re-implement in torch.** `crates/axc-driver/tests/common_q4km_f32ref.rs` is PROMOTED to a `pub` library module `axc_driver::q4km_oracle` and called from PyO3 via `q4km_f32_reference(q, x_bits, m, n, n_bpr) → (y_ref, abs_scale)`. The gate is the SAME backward-stable combined condition-aware metric the M3.x GPU tests (AT-1800/1801/1802) + the resident bench (AT-1805) use: `max_rel_diff_combined(gpu, y_ref, abs_scale) ≤` **FROZEN 1e-3**, element-local `|gpu−ref|/max(|ref|, Σ|wₖxₖ|)`. f32-accumulator semantics (dequant f32 → round each weight AND each x f32→f16→f32 → pure-f32 linear-k accumulate, the tight upper bound on f16×f16→f32 HMMA). A torch dequant would be a SECOND source of truth that drifts (f16 rounding, `get_scale_min_k4` bit-packing, accumulation order) and re-litigates the AT-1780 condition-number subtlety — so we call the IDENTICAL Rust code. The metric is computed in Rust (exported) so the FROZEN 1e-3 cannot be silently loosened in Python. **The 1e-3 NEVER loosens.** `raw_rel_diff` is reported for transparency only.

**AT-2105 — honest latency, no beat-cuBLAS claim.** Three CUDA-event min-of-N numbers at the A/B shape: (a) AXIOM zero-copy `matmul()` (dispatch+handshake, x already resident), (b) AXIOM host-copy baseline (CPU→.cuda()→shared x→matmul→C→.cpu(), the host round-trip zero-copy ELIMINATES), (c) native `torch.matmul` (cuBLAS, context only). The ONLY hard latency gate is **(a) < (b)** (the no-host-transfer win); (c) is REPORTED, NOT gated. Stated plainly: the Vulkan kernel is below cuBLAS (M3.6 42.86 TFLOPS; cuBLAS faster still), so end-to-end COMPUTE is SLOWER than native torch — the value is no-host-copy + real torch interop, not beating cuBLAS (R-6). *(Pre-measurement planning estimate was ~34% of a 125-TFLOPS datasheet figure; the MEASURED headline is **~53% of a same-machine f32 `torch.matmul` cuBLAS GEMM** — see the IMPLEMENTED result below. The two are NOT contradictory: 34% is vs a datasheet ESTIMATE, 53% is vs a measured f32 GEMM.)*

**Files.** NEW: `py/axiom_compute/q4km.py` (the Q4_K_M op + `pack_q4km` GGUF packer + the oracle driver), `py/tests/test_zero_copy_q4km.py` (AT-2103/2103b/2103c/2103d/2104-reuse/2105). MODIFIED: `crates/axc-py/src/lib.rs` (the `strategy` kwarg + `scalar_layout()` + the `q4km_f32_reference` bridge), `crates/axc-driver/src/lib.rs` (promote the oracle to `pub mod q4km_oracle`), `crates/axc-driver/Cargo.toml` (`half` → normal dependency), `py/axiom_compute/__init__.py` (thread `strategy` + `assemble_push_constants` + re-export). The handshake (V1/V2 timeline, same-stream, monotone), teardown (G-4), fail-closed UUID (AT-2107), and `dispatch_zero_copy` are Phase-1 VERBATIM — Phase 2 adds NO new sync.

**Honest acceptance gates (Phase 2).** HARD: AT-2103 (combined ≤ frozen 1e-3 at K=256/512/14336), AT-2103c (per-call path staging-free), AT-2104-reuse (≥100-call handshake determinism, bit-identical, monotone V1/V2), AT-2105 (zero-copy < host-copy; cuBLAS reported not gated), AT-2103d (fail-closed on CPU/wrong-dtype x and on strategy=None compile). HYGIENE: `cargo test` (default-members) green, maturin wheel rebuilds, Phase-1 G-item regressions still pass. Real GPU only (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000), no mocking. **Phase-1 residuals NOT fixed here (full fix M4.2, must not regress):** no timeout on CUDA data-path waits; NVIDIA-only EXCLUSIVE sync — Phase 2 adds no new sync, so these are inherited unchanged.

**Phase-2 IMPLEMENTED result (Coder, 2026-06-08, real NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, 580.x driver / CUDA 12.8 / torch 2.12).** The M3.6 Q4_K_M dequant+coopmat matmul runs as the HEADLINE op through the zero-copy frontend. **AT-2103 PASSES at all three depths** — the FROZEN combined condition-aware metric (computed in Rust via the promoted `axc_driver::q4km_oracle`) is far under 1e-3: **K=256 → combined=5.35e-7** (raw 2.55e-2, report-only), **K=512 → combined=6.71e-7** (raw 1.07e0), **K=14336 → combined=2.05e-6** (raw 2.10e0). K=14336 is the inference depth where the M3.5 f16 accumulator was INVALID (max_rel_diff ≈29); the f32 accumulator passes. The raw rel-error is the cancellation artifact (NOT the gate), logged for transparency. **AT-2103c**: 0 staging buffers / 0 `vkCmdCopyBuffer` across upload + ≥2 `matmul()` calls — the per-call x-in/C-out path is genuinely staging-free (q is the upload-once carve-out). **AT-2104**: 100 `matmul()` calls bit-identical (monotone V1/V2 handshake holds for the multi-buffer coopmat op). **AT-2103d**: `strategy=None` on the M3.6 source FAILS at compile (UnresolvedStrategyHole — the kwarg is load-bearing); wrong-dtype (f32) and CPU x fail closed (ZeroCopyUnavailable). **AT-2105 (honest)**: at m=4096,n=512,k=14336 — (a) AXIOM zero-copy **1.53 ms** (39.3 TFLOPS) **<** (b) AXIOM host-copy **2.95 ms** (the HARD gate: zero-copy eliminates the host round-trip, win 1.42 ms); (c) native `torch.matmul` f32 cuBLAS **0.81 ms** (74.7 TFLOPS, CONTEXT ONLY). AXIOM is ~53% of this f32-cuBLAS GEMM — **the Vulkan kernel is SLOWER than native torch.matmul; NO beat-cuBLAS claim** (cuBLAS is reported, not gated). Implemented: `crates/axc-driver/src/q4km_oracle.rs` (the test oracle PROMOTED verbatim to a `pub` lib module — single source of truth; the 9 `#[path]`-including tests/benches now `use axc_driver::q4km_oracle`, no duplicate compile), `half` → normal dependency; `crates/axc-py/src/lib.rs` (the optional `strategy` kwarg → `compile_source_with_assignments`, `scalar_layout()`, the `q4km_f32_reference`/`q4km_combined_metric`/`q4km_raw_rel_diff` bridges); `py/axiom_compute/q4km.py` (`Q4KMMatmul` + `pack_q4km` + the Rust-backed reference) + `py/axiom_compute/__init__.py` (`strategy` thread-through + `scalar_layout`/`assemble_push_constants` + re-export). `cargo test` (default-members) green: 915 passed / 0 failed (the oracle move did not regress); clippy `-D warnings` clean. The Phase-1 handshake/teardown/`dispatch_zero_copy` are reused VERBATIM — Phase 2 added NO new sync. Inherited residuals unchanged (no CUDA data-path wait timeout; NVIDIA-only EXCLUSIVE sync) — full fix M4.2.

*(Status: IMPLEMENTED — Architect (M4.1 Phase 2, dual design review APPROVE/APPROVE) → Coder. Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/M4.1p2-architect.json`; coder result → `.pipeline/runs/M4.1p2-coder.json`. Buffer plan: q upload-once (static Q4_K_M 144-byte superblocks) + x/C per-call zero-copy torch tensors; strategy-compile via an optional PyO3 `strategy` kwarg routing to the existing `compile_source_with_assignments`; oracle = the promoted `pub axc_driver::q4km_oracle` (single source of truth, FROZEN combined 1e-3 gate); AT-2105 latency reported honestly (no beat-cuBLAS). Ready for QA + dual code review.)*

> **[Phase-2 inherited residuals — UPDATED by Phase 3 (§3.1.27):** the *no-timeout on the CUDA data-path waits* residual is **CLOSED on BOTH sides** by Phase 3 — the bounded `wait_completion` (`AXC_FENCE_TIMEOUT_MS`) bounds the host wait, AND on a timeout the op host-signals the Vulkan timeline to V2 (`vkSignalSemaphore`) to release the dangling CUDA wait on stream `S` + poisons/rebuilds the session, so no CUDA wait is left enqueued forever; the *NVIDIA-only EXCLUSIVE-mode sync* residual remains open for M4.2 cross-vendor.]

## 3.1.27 M4.1 Phase 3 — `torch.library` custom-op registration + a ≤10-line demo + the wait-timeout residual fix [MERGED — completes the M4.1 ROADMAP scope]

Phase 1 (§3.1.25) proved the CUDA↔Vulkan zero-copy interop on saxpy; Phase 2 (§3.1.26) ran the M3.6 Q4_K_M dequant+coopmat matmul as the headline op through it (AT-2103 combined ≤ 1e-3 at K=256/512/14336; AT-2105 honest latency). **Phase 3 completes the M4.1 ROADMAP scope**: it makes that kernel a **first-class PyTorch operator** that **composes under `torch.compile`**, ships the **≤10-line user demo** the ROADMAP requires, and **closes the Phase-1/2 wait-timeout residual** — all with **NO codegen change**, **NO new sync machinery on the data path**, and **the FROZEN 1e-3 gate untouched**. Honest framing is unchanged: **no beat-cuBLAS** (~53% of f32 cuBLAS); the value is **real torch integration** (a registered `torch.ops.axiom.q4km_matmul` that Inductor/Dynamo can trace), not speed.

**The op schema + registration (`torch.library.custom_op`, torch 2.12).** The zero-copy Q4_K_M matmul registers as `torch.ops.axiom.q4km_matmul` with schema:

```
axiom::q4km_matmul(Tensor x, int weights_id, int M, int N, int K) -> Tensor
```

`x` is the f16 activation `[K, N]` row-major (the kernel reads `x[k*N+col]`); `weights_id` is a **stable integer handle** (NOT a Tensor, a **never-reused** monotone `u63` from `itertools.count` — UAF-safe; a released id fails closed) returned by `q4km_register_weights(packed_weights, M, K)` which uploads the static ggml Q4_K_M bytes **ONCE** into the shared `q` buffer (the Phase-2 carve-out, `q4km.py:246-301`) **and records `(M, K)` for the R1 validation below**. Passing the weights by `int` id — not a per-call Tensor — (a) keeps the kernel a clean **leaf** in the compiled graph (an int traces as a constant), and (b) prevents any temptation to re-upload weights per call. The output is a **fresh** `[M, N]` f32 tensor (a clone of the shared `C` view, see below). `custom_op(..., mutates_args=())` declares the op **functional** so `torch.compile` treats it as a pure leaf; a **`register_fake`** meta impl returns `x.new_empty((M, N), dtype=torch.float32)` (device = `x.device`) so Dynamo/FakeTensor traces the output metadata **without** running the Vulkan kernel or touching device memory. **No autograd** is registered (these are quantized-INFERENCE kernels): the demo/tests run under `torch.no_grad()`; a grad-tracking call raises honestly (never a silent wrong gradient).

**[R1 — caller `(M,N,K)` fail-closed validation so `register_fake` == the real clone, no `torch.compile` miscompile.]** The schema takes `M,N,K` as caller-supplied ints used in BOTH `register_fake` (`x.new_empty((M,N),f32)`) and the real impl, but the real impl returns a clone of the cached session's `[self._m, N]` C view (`q4km.py:375,378`) — so a wrong M (e.g. a `torch.compile` graph specialized on a different M constant) would make the fake meta `[M_caller,N]` diverge from the real `[self._m,N]` clone = a **silent miscompile**. **r2 fixes this:** the real CUDA impl, BEFORE any dispatch, performs an exact fail-closed check (raising `AxiomError`, NEVER silently returning a different shape): `M == entry.m`, `K == entry.k == x.shape[0]`, `N == x.shape[1]` (x must be a 2-D CUDA f16 tensor, reusing `q4km.py:331-344`). Only then does it resolve/create the `(weights_id, N)` session and dispatch, returning a `[M, N]` f32 clone on `x.device`. The PROOF that fake == real: `register_fake` produces `(M, N), f32, x.device`; the real impl asserts `M==entry.m`, `K==entry.k==x.shape[0]`, `N==x.shape[1]`, then clones the `[entry.m, N]==[M,N]` C view as f32 on `x.device` — provably identical `(shape, dtype, device)`. Any `(M,N,K)` inconsistent with the weights_id's compiled kernel + `x.shape` **raises** (fail-closed). `register_fake` still does NOT touch the registry (FakeTensorMode has no device memory). **AT-2111c** passes a mismatched M (then N, then K) and asserts the op RAISES, never a silent clone.

**SharedSession state-caching — the load-bearing design.** From torch's view the op is stateless `(x, weights_id, M, N, K) → C`, but the AXIOM side needs **persistent** exportable buffers (q uploaded once; x/C imported into CUDA once). Re-creating a `Q4KMMatmul`/`SharedSession` per call would **re-export** the Vulkan buffers, **re-import** them into CUDA, and **re-upload** q every call — slower than a host copy and an fd-churn. So the op **caches**: a process-local registry keyed by `weights_id` holds one `Q4KMMatmul` (compiled once, q uploaded once) plus one `SharedSession` **per N** (a second N constructs an additional `Q4KMMatmul` re-using the same q bytes — an honest, amortized new-session cost, not a per-call cost; this leaves `q4km.py`'s single-N-per-instance rule unchanged). The registry `threading.Lock` is **held across the WHOLE op body** (lookup → R1-validate → ensure-session → dispatch → `wait_completion` → clone → cross-stream hand-back), NOT just the dict access — so a concurrent `q4km_release_weights` can NEVER close a session mid-dispatch (it takes the same lock and blocks until the in-flight call completes). `q4km_release_weights(weights_id)` runs the exact G-4 teardown and drops the entry (idempotent; weights_id never reused); an `atexit` hook releases all entries **best-effort** (swallowing errors — torch's CUDA context may already be torn down at shutdown; the PRIMARY teardown is the explicit call). **AT-2113b** asserts the session identity is stable across ≥100 op calls (0/0 staging), proving the amortization — a re-create-per-call regression would change session identity and churn fds.

**[mutates_args=() soundness invariant.]** The op is declared functional yet mutates op-PRIVATE shared scratch (the persistent `C` buffer every call; the shared `x` view on the device_copy path). This is invisible to torch — and Inductor CSE/dedup of two identical-arg calls is sound — ONLY because (a) the registry lock held across the whole body guarantees **no two op calls on the same `(weights_id,N)` session are in-flight concurrently**, and (b) the clone is ordered strictly AFTER `wait_completion` on the session stream (R3), so the clone captures the result before any next call can overwrite `C`. The op is therefore a **deterministic function of `(x` contents`, weights)`**. **AT-2117** runs `torch.library.opcheck`; **AT-2111e** is a fullgraph dedup test (call the op TWICE with the same x object + args in a compiled region; assert BOTH outputs correct — Inductor did not collapse to one stale clone). (A future `out=` variant returning the shared view would make `mutates_args=()` a lie — Phase 3 returns a clone, so it is sound.)

**Zero-copy fast path vs arbitrary-tensor copy path (HONEST, EAGER-scoped).** A registered op receives an **arbitrary** user tensor, so Phase 3 reuses Phase-2's already-resolved split (`q4km.py:318-376`): **(a) zero-copy fast path (EAGER)** when `x` IS the axiom-allocated contiguous `[K, N]` shared view (`q4km_activation_view(weights_id, N)`) — no copy, kernel reads x directly, `last_dispatch_path()=='zero_copy'`, `staging==0 / copies==0` (AT-2112); **(b) device-copy path** for any other CUDA f16 `[K, N]` tensor (the common compiled-graph case) — a device-to-device copy into the shared view (no host round-trip), `last_dispatch_path()=='device_copy'`, **honestly labeled, never claimed zero-copy** (AT-2113). The W1 guard (`q4km.py:351-366`) still **rejects** a non-contiguous/transposed same-storage view rather than silently reinterpret memory. The op returns a **clone** of `C` (not the persistent shared view) so a subsequent op call (which overwrites `C`) cannot torn-read a value already handed to the graph; the clone is one disclosed `[M, N]` device copy. **[Honest scope of the zero-copy claim.]** Under fullgraph `torch.compile`, Inductor **functionalization** may realize/clone the input `x` before the op, so `x.data_ptr()` identity at the op boundary may be LOST even when the user wrote into the shared `x_view` → the `zero_copy` fast path may be **unreachable under compile** (it correctly + silently falls to `device_copy`). The 0/0-staging zero-copy claim is therefore an **EAGER / advanced-user** property; under `torch.compile` the op is CORRECT but may take the device_copy path. AT-2112 is an EAGER test; AT-2111b does NOT assert zero_copy under compile — it asserts correctness + records whichever path ran.

**`torch.compile` composition.** The op composes because it is `custom_op`-registered with a schema, `register_fake` supplies output metadata (provably == the real clone shape after R1), and `mutates_args=()` marks it pure (no graph break, the soundness invariant above holds). **AT-2111b** compiles `f(x)=torch.ops.axiom.q4km_matmul(x, wid, M, N, K)` with `torch.compile(..., fullgraph=True)` under `torch.no_grad()`, runs it on the GPU, and asserts **no graph break** (the op is a single leaf node, checked via the fx graph / `torch._dynamo` explain), **compiled output == eager output**, and **combined metric ≤ FROZEN 1e-3** (recording whichever dispatch path ran). **AT-2117** runs `torch.library.opcheck` for schema/fake/impl consistency.

**[R3 — execute on the session stream + cross-stream hand-back, not a bare warning.]** The cached `SharedSession` captures ONE stream `S` at construction. Under `torch.compile` the op is called from torch's **current** stream, which may DIFFER from `S` → a silent torn read if the data ops merely ran there (the Phase-1 `_assert_captured_stream` was only a `warnings.warn`). **r2 enforces stream consistency:** the op executes its handshake + device copy + `cv.clone()` INSIDE `with torch.cuda.stream(session.stream):`, with correct cross-stream ordering — if the caller's stream differs from `S`, it records an event `ev_in` on the caller's stream and `S.wait_event(ev_in)` (so `S` sees the caller's prior writes to `x` before the kernel reads it), and after the clone records `ev_out` on `S` and `caller_stream.wait_event(ev_out)` (so the returned clone is visible back on the caller's stream). The clone runs ON `S`, ordered strictly AFTER `wait_completion` (so it captures the kernel's `C` write, never a torn value). The result is correct regardless of torch's active stream — a warning is replaced by enforcement. **AT-2111d** runs the op from a different active CUDA stream than `S` (and under `torch.compile`) and asserts bit-exact correctness.

**The ≤10-line demo** (`py/examples/q4km_torch_compile_demo.py`, ROADMAP M4.1 "10-line PyTorch user code"): import → `q4km_register_weights(q, M, K)` → an f16 activation → `torch.compile` a one-line lambda calling the op → `with torch.no_grad(): C = f(x)` → print shape + `last_dispatch_path`. **AT-2116** runs it on the GPU and asserts `C` matches the Rust oracle within FROZEN 1e-3.

**The wait-timeout residual fix (closes it on BOTH sides).** Phase 1/2's CUDA data-path waits (`_cuda_interop.py:167-176` `cudaWaitExternalSemaphoresAsync`) are **unbounded**; the Vulkan compute submit (`context.rs:1087-1128`) has **no host fence** by design. If a dispatch errors after one side signaled, the other side's wait — surfaced later via `stream.synchronize()` — could **hang forever**. The Vulkan-side bounds **already exist**: the `dispatch_zero_copy` pre-reset UAF wait (`context.rs:1021-1034`) and `drain_external_timeline` (`context.rs:1141-1165`) are both bounded by `fence_timeout_ms`. Phase 3 closes the **completion** gap with a new **`SharedSession.wait_completion(timeout_ms=None)`** — a **bounded** host drain of the timeline to the last `V2` (via the new PyO3 `drain_with_timeout` → `VulkanContext::drain_external_timeline_with_timeout`, default `AXC_FENCE_TIMEOUT_MS`=10 000 ms). The **registered op uses it as its owned-output completion barrier** (replacing an unbounded `stream.synchronize()` before cloning `C`) — a host sync is **already** implied by returning a concrete tensor, so this adds **no overhead there**. The **bare `SharedSession.run` data path is UNCHANGED** (still `signal V1 → dispatch → wait V2` on stream S, **no host fence, no new sync**, G-5 intact); **AT-2115** asserts the fast path is unchanged.

**[R2 — bounding the HOST wait alone leaves the CUDA side poisoned; r2 releases it.]** Bounding `wait_completion` surfaces a stalled handshake as a Python exception instead of an unbounded `cudaStreamSynchronize` hang — but in the **GPU-fault-mid-dispatch** case (the Vulkan submit succeeded, `V1` signaled, the CUDA `cudaWaitExternalSemaphoresAsync(V2)` is **already enqueued on stream `S`**, then the kernel faults so `V2` never signals), the CUDA wait(V2) stays enqueued + **unbounded** → stream `S` is **POISONED** (the next CUDA op on `S` deadlocks). `vkDeviceWaitIdle` drains the Vulkan queue but does NOT satisfy a pending CUDA external-semaphore wait. **r2 fix (release THEN rebuild):** on a `wait_completion` timeout (or any dispatch error after `V1` signaled), the op (1) **releases the dangling CUDA wait** by HOST-SIGNALING the shared Vulkan timeline to `V2` — `session.release_cuda_wait(V2)` → PyO3 `host_signal_timeline(V2)` → `VulkanContext::host_signal_external_timeline(V2)` = `vkSignalSemaphore(VkSemaphoreSignalInfo{ semaphore=external_timeline, value=V2 })`; because the Vulkan timeline IS the same semaphore CUDA imported, host-signaling it to `V2` **satisfies** `cudaWaitExternalSemaphoresAsync(V2)` on stream `S` → `S` **unblocks** (no CUDA op left hanging); (2) **then marks the session POISONED + evicts the cache entry** (`session.poison()`), so the faulted session + its stream `S` are NEVER reused — the next op call for `(weights_id, N)` **rebuilds** a fresh `Q4KMMatmul`/`SharedSession` (+ a fresh stream); (3) **then surfaces the error** to Python (`AxiomError`). Doing BOTH is deliberate: host-signaling `V2` unblocks any already-enqueued CUDA op on `S` right now; poison+rebuild guarantees a possibly-still-mid-flight / device-lost session is not reused for a new dispatch. The timeout now bounds **both** the host AND the CUDA side — no path leaves a CUDA wait enqueued forever. **AT-2114 (r2 strengthened)** ENQUEUES the **real** CUDA wait(V2) on stream `S` via the op's run path, then never GPU-signals `V2`, and asserts (a) `wait_completion(timeout_ms=small)` **raises** within ~small ms (NOT a hang), (b) a **SUBSEQUENT CUDA op on stream `S` does NOT hang** (proving `S` was recovered, not merely the host call bounded), and (c) a fresh op call on the rebuilt session succeeds — in an **isolated** session with a **hard wall-clock watchdog** (<3 s) so a true hang fails the suite. Default + override reuse `AXC_FENCE_TIMEOUT_MS` — **no new env var, no codegen change** (`vkSignalSemaphore` is an existing Vulkan host op, not codegen).

*(Status: DELIVERED — Coder (M4.1 Phase 3, **r2** spec; both design reviewers APPROVED). Implemented on `coder/M41p3-20260608/M4.1-torch-library-op`: `py/axiom_compute/ops.py` (the registered op + the `(weights_id,N)` registry), `SharedSession.wait_completion/release_cuda_wait/poison` (`__init__.py`), the Rust `drain_external_timeline_with_timeout` + `host_signal_external_timeline` + `fence_timeout_ms()` (`context.rs`) surfaced via PyO3 `drain_with_timeout`/`host_signal_timeline`/`fence_timeout_ms` (`lib.rs`), the ≤10-line demo + AT-2111..2118. GPU-verified on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (580.x): AT-2111b `torch.compile(fullgraph=True)` graph_break_count=0 (combined 5.3e-7, path=device_copy — zero-copy EAGER-only); AT-2111c/d/e + AT-2117 opcheck pass; **AT-2114 PASS — host `vkSignalSemaphore` on a faulted-GPU timeline releases the dangling CUDA wait(V2) so stream S unblocks (the both-sides deadlock fix works on 580.x)**; AT-2115 happy path unchanged; AT-2116 demo correct. NO codegen change, FROZEN 1e-3 untouched, no beat-cuBLAS. Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/M4.1p3-architect.json`. Op schema `axiom::q4km_matmul(Tensor x, int weights_id, int M, int N, int K) -> Tensor` via `torch.library.custom_op` + `register_fake` (shape-only meta) + a CUDA impl driving a `(weights_id, N)`-keyed cached `SharedSession`; honest zero-copy-vs-device-copy split with path reporting; `torch.compile(fullgraph=True)` composition; a ≤10-line demo; the wait-timeout residual closed on BOTH sides. **r2 fixes: (R1)** the real impl FAIL-CLOSED-VALIDATES caller `(M,N,K)` vs the weights record + `x.shape` (`M==entry.m`, `K==entry.k==x.shape[0]`, `N==x.shape[1]`) before any dispatch, so `register_fake` (`x.new_empty((M,N),f32)`) and the real clone are PROVABLY identical `(shape,dtype,device)` — a mismatch RAISES, never a silent miscompile (AT-2111c); **(R2)** on a `wait_completion` timeout / dispatch error the op RELEASES the dangling CUDA wait(V2) by host-signaling the Vulkan timeline to V2 (`vkSignalSemaphore`) so stream `S` unblocks, THEN POISONS + rebuilds the session (never reuses a faulted stream) — bounding BOTH the host and the CUDA side; AT-2114 enqueues the REAL CUDA wait(V2), never GPU-signals it, and asserts a subsequent CUDA op on `S` does not hang, with a watchdog in an isolated session; **(R3)** the op EXECUTES its handshake + device copy + clone inside `with torch.cuda.stream(session.stream)` with a cross-stream event hand-back (`ev_in` caller→S, `ev_out` S→caller), correct regardless of torch's active stream under `torch.compile` (AT-2111d) — not a bare warning. **Folded refinements:** the registry lock spans the WHOLE op body (release cannot close a session mid-dispatch); the zero-copy claim is EAGER-scoped (Inductor functionalization may force device_copy under compile — disclosed); the no-two-concurrent-in-flight-per-session invariant is stated (what makes `mutates_args=()` sound) with `opcheck` (AT-2117) + a fullgraph dedup test (AT-2111e); weights_id is a never-reused monotone `u63` with explicit `q4km_release_weights` (G-4) + best-effort `atexit`. NO codegen change, FROZEN 1e-3 untouched, no beat-cuBLAS. AT-2111..AT-2118 (r2 adds AT-2111c R1, AT-2111d R3, AT-2111e dedup; AT-2114 strengthened for R2). Ready for Coder.)*
