**The ≤15-line demo** (`py/examples/sdpa_torch_compile_backend_demo.py`): a tiny non-causal attention `nn.Module` using `F.scaled_dot_product_attention`; `import axiom_compute as axc` registers `backend="axiom"`; `axc.reset_axiom_sdpa_counters()`; `out = torch.compile(model, backend="axiom")(x)`; `assert axc.axiom_sdpa_runtime_fire_count() > 0` (the AXIOM op FIRED at runtime — distinct from the compile-time node-swap counter `axiom_sdpa_dispatch_count()`); `assert torch.allclose(out, model(x), atol=1e-3, rtol=1e-3)`. Prints "AXIOM flash_attention fired N time(s); max diff vs eager = …; (capability demo — SLOWER than native SDPA, opt-in)".

**Acceptance (HONEST; real NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, Lavapipe coopmat-typed-skip for the GPU legs).** **AT-2140** `backend="axiom"` registered + idempotent (CPU-ok). **AT-2141** the op FIRES on a matching `[1,1,512,64]` SDPA (`axiom_sdpa_dispatch_count()>0` AND `axiom_sdpa_runtime_fire_count()>0`). **AT-2142** output within FROZEN 1e-3 of eager (expected ~5–9e-5; near-1e-3 ⇒ report the f16 limitation, never loosen). **AT-2143** within 1e-3 of the default Inductor backend. **AT-2144** non-matching SDPA (head_dim≠64 / seq%16≠0 / causal-KWARG / **causal-POSITIONAL `F.sdpa(q,k,v,None,0.0,True)`** / masked-KWARG / **masked-POSITIONAL `F.sdpa(q,k,v,mask)`** / f16 / custom scale / **GQA `q[2,8,512,64]` vs `kv[2,2,512,64]`**) falls back cleanly — `fire_count==0`, output==eager, NO break/error. **AT-2145** a mixed Linear→ReLU→SDPA→Linear model lowers ONLY the SDPA. **AT-2146** `[2,4,512,64]` multi-head lowers via the per-head loop (`fire_count==B*H==8`), output within 1e-3. **AT-2147** the ≤15-line demo runs on GPU, the op fires, output within 1e-3. **AT-2148** NO regression to the explicit `flash_attention`/`q4km` ops + a no-SDPA model is a pure Inductor passthrough (`fire_count==0`, output==`backend="inductor"`). **AT-2149 (CPU, no GPU)** a unit test of `_get_sdpa_arg` + `_sdpa_match` over the full positional/keyword cross product (positional causal, positional mask, kwarg causal, custom scale, GQA shapes-disagree, unknown form, the matching f32/D==64/S%16==0 case, the `example_value`-only meta case) asserts the match/no-match decision DIRECTLY — catches the positional-form silent-wrong-math hole on Lavapipe/CPU CI. **HONEST: a CAPABILITY demo, NOT perf** — auto-routing to AXIOM attention is SLOWER than native SDPA; OPT-IN only; NO beat claim; q4km HONESTLY out of auto-lowering scope. NO codegen/runtime change (Python-only). FROZEN 1e-3 NEVER loosens. Real GPU verification.

**Top risks.** **R-A** the SDPA representation at the backend boundary — if torch 2.12 already decomposed SDPA before the backend, the single-node match finds nothing; MITIGATION: match the functional AND the aten overload, the Coder SPIKES the actual node target on a tiny model FIRST, honest fallback = match only the non-decomposed form (reported, not a silent miss). **R-B** the `[B,H,S,D]` per-head loop (a wrong select/stack permutes heads, silent wrong output — caught by AT-2146's 1e-3 gate). **R-C** the Inductor-fallback delegation on a mutated graph (mitigated by `lint()`+`recompile()`, the op's `register_fake` meta, AT-2145, the `gm.forward` hard fallback). **R-D** match robustness — a false positive lowering a causal/masked/scaled SDPA is silent wrong math. The PRECISE hole (pessimistic spike, CLOSED in r2): `is_causal`/`attn_mask` are POSITIONAL-OR-KEYWORD and can arrive ENTIRELY POSITIONALLY (args[5]/args[3]) with empty kwargs, so a kwargs-only reader would miss them. MITIGATION (r2): the single fail-closed `_get_sdpa_arg` reads each positional-or-keyword flag `args[idx] if present else kwarg`, each keyword-only flag (scale/enable_gqa) from kwargs only, with a P0 unknown-form fallback (`len(args)>6` / unknown kwarg key / unknown target ⇒ no match); the predicate is otherwise CONSERVATIVE; AT-2144 enumerates non-matches INCLUDING the positional causal/mask + GQA forms, and AT-2149 unit-tests the extractor+predicate on CPU. **R-E** the honest "slower than native" framing (mitigated by DESIGN + demo + docstrings stating capability-not-perf, opt-in, no beat). **R-F** q4km mis-scoped as auto-lowerable (mitigated by the explicit out-of-scope statement — no Q4_K_M graph pattern exists). **R-G** Lavapipe coopmat-unsupported in CI (the GPU legs run on NVIDIA; Lavapipe is a typed skip; AT-2140 + the predicate-unit logic run on CPU).

*(Status: **MERGED (Coder, spike-first; r2 design APPROVED by both reviewers)** — Architect (M4.3, **r2** after dual design review: optimistic APPROVE, pessimistic NEEDS_REVISION — ONE blocker, the SDPA flag extraction must be provably fail-closed against the POSITIONAL form, else a positionally-causal/masked SDPA is silently lowered to FULL attention = wrong math). Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/M43-architect.json` (verdict PROPOSED first key). **r2 fixes:** **(BLOCKER)** the flag extraction is now a single fail-closed helper `_get_sdpa_arg` — each POSITIONAL-OR-KEYWORD flag (attn_mask=idx3, dropout_p=idx4, is_causal=idx5) read `args[idx] if len(args)>idx else kwargs.get(name, default)`, each KEYWORD-ONLY flag (scale, enable_gqa) from kwargs only, plus a P0 unknown-form fallback (`len(args)>6` / unknown kwarg key / unknown target ⇒ no match) — so a positionally-causal `F.sdpa(q,k,v,None,0.0,True)` (is_causal at args[5]) is READ and rejected, never silently lowered. **(GQA)** the FULL-4-tuple shapes-agree check (q.shape==k.shape==v.shape) is FOREGROUNDED as the PRIMARY GQA/MQA guard (Hkv<Hq fails ⇒ no match). **(META-KEY)** the meta read is pinned to `['example_value','val','tensor_meta']` (example_value FIRST — it is the FakeTensor at the Dynamo backend boundary; val/tensor_meta absent there), fail-closed to no-match if all absent. **(TESTS)** AT-2144 gains positional-causal + positional-mask + GQA non-match fixtures; AT-2149 adds a CPU-only unit test of the extractor+predicate over the positional/keyword cross product. The M4 ADOPTION CAPSTONE: a `torch._dynamo.register_backend("axiom", axiom_backend)` custom backend that walks the Dynamo FX graph, pattern-matches `scaled_dot_product_attention` nodes satisfying the AXIOM kernel's EXACT constraints (non-causal, no mask, dropout 0, default scale, f32, D==64, S%16==0, full-4-tuple shapes-agree, static shapes, not requires_grad, `B*H<=AXIOM_SDPA_MAX_HEADS`), rewrites ONLY those to per-head `torch.ops.axiom.flash_attention` calls (the `[B,H,S,D]`→`[S,D]` loop: reshape→select→contiguous→op→stack→reshape), and DELEGATES the rewritten graph to the default Inductor backend (`torch._inductor.compile`) so all non-matching ops fall back cleanly — no graph break, no error, no behavior change. PYTHON-ONLY (no Rust/`.axc`/SPIR-V/codegen/runtime change): a backend fn + an FX pass REUSING the merged `torch.ops.axiom.flash_attention` op (`attention_ops.py:207-300`, `register_fake` `:215-219`). Correctness anchor = FROZEN combined 1e-3 vs eager AND the default Inductor backend (the op's inherited ~5–9e-5 numerics, §3.1.31/§3.1.32 — never loosened). New artifacts: `py/axiom_compute/compile_backend.py` (the backend + `_get_sdpa_arg`/`_read_meta_tensor`/`_sdpa_match` + `_rewrite_sdpa_to_axiom` + the dispatch/runtime-fire counters), `py/examples/sdpa_torch_compile_backend_demo.py` (≤15-line demo), `py/tests/test_torch_compile_backend.py` (AT-2140..2149); `__init__.py` re-exports + an import-time `register_axiom_backend()` call. HONEST: a CAPABILITY demonstration (AXIOM as a torch.compile backend auto-lowering a matching op, correct) — NOT a perf win (SLOWER than native SDPA), OPT-IN only, NO beat claim; q4km HONESTLY out of auto-lowering scope (needs a pre-packed int weights_id, not a graph value). FROZEN 1e-3 untouched. Real GPU verification. Both reviewers' non-blocking notes folded; ready for Coder (spike-first). AT-2140..AT-2149.)*

**Coder result (r2-coder, 2026-06-10).** STEP-0 spike RE-CONFIRMED the r2 pins on the **in-env torch (2.9.1+cu128 — the actual installed torch, NOT 2.12; matches the design's 2.9.1 spike box; no API delta on the stable register_backend / inductor.compile / fx surfaces)**: the SDPA node target at the Dynamo backend boundary is `<built-in function scaled_dot_product_attention>` == `F.scaled_dot_product_attention` == `torch._C._nn.scaled_dot_product_attention` (single `call_function` node, NOT decomposed); the FakeTensor meta lives in `node.meta['example_value']` (`val`/`tensor_meta` ABSENT — `example_value` tried FIRST); a positional-causal `F.sdpa(q,k,v,None,0.0,True)` arrives as `args=(q,k,v,None,0.0,True)` with empty kwargs (is_causal at `args[5]`), a positional mask as `args[3]` (an FX Node). Built `py/axiom_compute/compile_backend.py` (the `axiom_backend` + the fail-closed `_get_sdpa_arg`/`_read_meta_tensor`/`_sdpa_match`/`_static_shape` + the per-head `_rewrite_sdpa_to_axiom` + the two counters + a runtime-fire **counting-shim registered op** `torch.ops.axiom.flash_attention_counted_shim` so the runtime fire survives Inductor as an opaque extern call), the ≤15-line demo `py/examples/torch_compile_axiom_backend_demo.py`, and `py/tests/test_torch_compile_backend.py` (AT-2140..AT-2149); `__init__.py` re-exports + an import-time `register_axiom_backend()`. **AT-2149 (CPU, no GPU) PASSES** — the positional-causal / positional-mask / kwarg-causal / custom-scale / GQA-shapes-disagree / unknown-form fixtures all return NO MATCH; the f32/D==64/S%16==0/`example_value`-only case MATCHES; a kwargs-only reader would have matched the positional-causal fixture and failed the assert. **GPU (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000) PASSES:** the AXIOM op FIRES on a matching `[1,1,512,64]` SDPA (`dispatch>0` AND `runtime_fire>0`), output within FROZEN 1e-3 of eager (measured max diff ~1.5e-4–2e-4) AND of `backend="inductor"`; every non-matching case (head_dim≠64 / seq%16≠0 / causal-KWARG / **causal-POSITIONAL** / masked-KWARG / **masked-POSITIONAL** / f16 / custom-scale / **GQA** / non-SDPA) falls back cleanly (`runtime_fire==0`, no break/error); the `[2,4,512,64]` multi-head case fires `B*H==8` times with DISTINCT per-head data within 1e-3 (no head permutation); the demo fires + within 1e-3; no regression to the explicit `flash_attention`/`q4km` ops + a no-SDPA model is a pure Inductor passthrough. **Test counts:** Python full suite 81 passed / 0 failed (33 in the new M4.3 file: AT-2149 CPU falsifier + AT-2140 registration run on CPU, the rest on GPU); `cargo check`/`clippy -- -D warnings`/`test --workspace` clean & green (Python-only change, no Rust touched). **Test-hygiene note (risk R-H):** the fire counters are advisory process-global hooks; Dynamo caches a compiled artifact per `forward` CODE OBJECT, so the test file uses an autouse `torch._dynamo.reset()` + counter-zero fixture per test (without it a second `torch.compile` of the same module class reuses the cached guards and the backend is not re-invoked). **Deviations:** (1) demo/test filenames are `torch_compile_axiom_backend_demo.py` / `test_torch_compile_backend.py` (per the Coder task naming, vs the design's `sdpa_*` names) — no behavior change. (2) the runtime-fire counter is a REGISTERED custom-op shim (`flash_attention_counted_shim`) rather than an `allow_in_graph` plain function, the cleaner of the architect's two open-question options, so it deterministically survives Inductor. (3) the flash-attention kernel `.axc` is NOT bundled in the abi3 wheel (PRE-EXISTING — only the q4km kernel is in `kernels/`; the flash kernel resolves via the repo `examples/` dir in the source-tree layout, same as the existing M4.1-Phase-4 flash demo) — so the GPU demo/tests run from the source tree, unchanged by this Python-only milestone. **No escalation** (no codegen/runtime/kernel change). HONEST: capability demo, SLOWER than native SDPA, opt-in, no beat claim; q4km out of auto-lowering scope; FROZEN 1e-3 held.)*

## 3.1.34 M3.2c-optC — Option C feasibility: full-coopmat acc via diagonal-scale is BLOCKED by the KHR coopmat use-type system [HONEST-NEGATIVE, design-level]

The prior coopmat-attention milestones (§3.1.30 M3.2c-perf, §3.1.32 M3.2c-PV) accelerated BOTH matmuls (Q·Kᵀ AND P·V) on tensor cores but kept the FlashAttention output accumulator `acc` in `shared[f32,16*64]` with a SCALAR per-row rescale-add (`acc_sh[c]=acc_sh[c]*corr_sh[r]+pv_sh[c]`, `flash_attention_coopmat_pv.axc:258-263`), because the online-softmax rescales `acc` by a per-row `correction` each K/V block and `coopmat_mul_add(A,B,C)=A·B+C` has NO per-row/diagonal scale. Each prior milestone explicitly **DEFERRED "Option C"**: make `acc` a loop-carried Accumulator-use coopmat and fold the rescale into a coopmat op via a diagonal matrix — `acc = diag(correction) @ acc + P·V == coopmat_mul_add(D, acc, PV)`, with `D[i][i]=correction[i]`, `D[i][j]=0`. **This milestone RESOLVES Option C: it is not deferred — it is BLOCKED by the VK_KHR_cooperative_matrix use-type system.** No register-resident full-coopmat-acc attention is expressible on the portable KHR coopmat extension. **The decisive reason (verified against the typechecker, not just the spec): `coopmat_mul_add(A,B,C)` requires A=MatrixA-use, B=MatrixB-use, C/result=Accumulator-use — HARD-enforced (`typecheck.rs:2982-2998` AUseMismatch/BUseMismatch/CUseMismatch) — AND it ALWAYS yields an Accumulator-use result (`coopmat.rs:186`). So even if one declared the running `acc` as a B-use matrix, the instant it passes through a single `coopmat_mul_add` it becomes Accumulator-use and the next K/V iteration can no longer feed it as the B-operand — the FlashAttention recurrence has NO FIXED POINT under the KHR use-type algebra. There is no use-conversion op to recover, and re-folding the rescale onto P or V is mathematically wrong (the per-row `correction` scales the ACCUMULATED history, not the current block).**

**Sub-question that DOES work (pure source): the diagonal tile.** `D` can be built WITHOUT a new builtin: a 16×16 f16 shared tile `diag_sh`, scalar-zeroed (the existing staging-loop pattern), with `diag_sh[r*16+r]=f32_to_f16(corr_sh[r])` on the diagonal (lane r<16), a barrier, then `coopmat_load(diag_sh,0u32,16u32)` as an A-use matrix — the SAME shared A-load path as `p_sh` (`emit_coopmat_load_shared_inline`, codegen `coopmat.rs:315`, AT-1614). The j==0 case is FREE: the existing `is_first` guard forces `corr_sh[r]=0` (`flash_attention_coopmat_pv.axc:197-200`) ⇒ `diag(0)` is the zero matrix ⇒ `coopmat_mul_add(D,acc,PV)=PV`, i.e. "first block: acc=PV", WITHOUT a conditional coopmat reassign (the j==0 special-casing lives entirely in the SCALAR diag-fill; `acc` is reassigned unconditionally at the K/V top level — the AT-1708 conditional-reassign trap is avoided by construction).

**The BLOCKER (the load-bearing negative).** `OpCooperativeMatrixMulAddKHR` computes A·B+C with FIXED operand uses: A→MatrixAKHR (0), B→MatrixBKHR (1), C and result→MatrixAccumulatorKHR (2) (codegen `coopmat.rs:71-75`; HIR `axc-hir/src/coopmat.rs:53-56`). To evaluate `diag(corr)@acc` as `coopmat_mul_add(D, acc, PV)`, the loop-carried `acc` must be the **B operand ⇒ MatrixBKHR-use** — but `acc` is the OUTPUT accumulator ⇒ **MatrixAccumulatorKHR-use**. These are DISTINCT opaque `OpTypeCooperativeMatrixKHR` types (the Use parameter differs; emitted at `coopmat.rs:124-138`). The KHR coopmat ext has **NO instruction to convert/reinterpret an Accumulator-use matrix into a B-use (or A-use) matrix in registers** — the type is OPAQUE (element layout is implementation-defined across the subgroup's lanes; `OpCooperativeMatrixLengthKHR` gives length, not a general writeable element accessor). AXIOM exposes only FOUR coopmat builtins — Zero/Load/Store/MulAdd (HIR `coopmat.rs:169-172`); there is **NO `coopmat_convert`/`coopmat_recast`** (grep across crates: none). The symmetric attempt `acc·D` fails identically (acc would need MatrixAKHR-use). **The ONLY way to make the Accumulator-use `acc` available as a B-use operand is to round-trip it through memory** — `coopmat_store(acc → acc_stage_sh)` → barrier → `coopmat_load(acc_stage_sh) as B-use f16` — which (1) RE-INTRODUCES the exact shared round-trip Option C aimed to delete (now the WHOLE [16×64] acc, 4× the per-block traffic of just storing PV), AND (2) NARROWS the f32 accumulator to f16 EVERY K/V block. M3.5b (§3.1.21) proved an f16 accumulator is numerically INVALID at large K (max_rel_diff≈29). So Option C as a register-resident win is IMPOSSIBLE, and as a buildable kernel it is both SLOWER (more shared traffic + a loop-carried coopmat across the whole attention loop) AND less accurate (repeated f16 narrowing of `acc`) than M3.2c-PV.

**Why M3.2c-PV's scalar rescale is actually the right design.** Keeping `acc` in f32 SHARED is exactly where the per-row diagonal scale is a cheap per-element f32 multiply with full precision. The coopmat machinery has no per-row/diagonal scale primitive precisely because that is NOT a tensor-core operation; forcing it onto tensor cores via a diagonal MMA is a pessimization (a full 16×16×16 multiply to do 16 scalar multiplies) plus an accuracy loss.

**Decision: PROPOSED as an HONEST-NEGATIVE design record — NO new codegen feature, NO leader change.** The diagonal tile is pure-source; the blocker is NOT an AXIOM gap but a SPIR-V/Vulkan extension constraint. The **M3.2c-PV scalar-rescale acc-in-shared kernel REMAINS the FlashAttention leader.** An OPTIONAL empirical falsifier (`examples/flash_attention_coopmat_optc.axc` = M3.2c-PV + the acc store/reload round-trip + diag-tile + `coopmat_mul_add(D, acc_b, PV)`, acc loop-carried via the M3.6 single-level OpPhi) MAY be built to PROVE the negative on real GPU (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000) — EXPECTED slower than M3.2c-PV AND possibly breaching FROZEN 1e-3 at seq=2048 from the repeated f16-acc narrowing — but the paper proof of the type-system blocker already suffices; build it only if a reviewer disputes the blocker. FROZEN 1e-3 NEVER loosens. **Future-portability flag (out of scope):** VK_NV_cooperative_matrix2 adds `coopMatReduceNV`/a per-element op that COULD do a per-row scale on an accumulator in registers — but it is NV-only (breaks the portability thesis) and is a separate milestone. Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/M32coptC-architect.json` (verdict PROPOSED first key). AT-2230 (the paper feasibility finding, MANDATORY) + AT-2231..2234 (the optional GPU falsifier) + AT-2235 (M3.2c-PV no-regression).

---

## 3.1.35 EB.2 — Per-machine bench-regression baselines [MERGED]

**Problem.** The bench-regression harness keys baselines by `(group,bench)` only, inside one flat `BaselineFile` (schema v1: `bench_regression.rs:43-56`, `postprocess.rs:36-66`). The blessed file carries a `machine` meta object, but the GATE never reads it (`bench_regression.rs:202-248` iterates `benchmarks` with no machine matching): last-machine-to-bless-wins, and the 15% gate compares the current run against whatever machine blessed last. With one machine (NVIDIA) this is benign. The instant AMD/Intel hardware lands (**EB.1**), an AMD `AXC_BLESS_BASELINES=1` overwrites the NVIDIA numbers (`postprocess.rs:331-343` writes the single file) and the gate compares AMD-vs-NVIDIA — a meaningless regression signal. This is the same class of bug ROADMAP.md:311/EB.4 names (Lavapipe runs overwriting NVIDIA and vice-versa). **EB.2 makes the baseline per-machine so each machine keeps its own blessed numbers and the gate is same-machine-vs-same-machine.**

**Machine key.** Primary source = the Vulkan `deviceName` via `VulkanContext::physical_device_name()` (`context.rs:1352`) — the stable, human-readable GPU identity (e.g. `NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Workstation Edition`, `AMD Radeon 780M`, `Intel(R) Arc(tm) A770`), queried at bench time from the SAME device the kernel runs on. `sanitize(s)`: lowercase ASCII, map non-`[a-z0-9]`→`_`, collapse runs of `_`, trim, cap 80 — yielding a valid JSON key / filesystem token. NVIDIA key = `nvidia_rtx_pro_6000_blackwell_workstation_edition`. **EB.2 decides device-name-only as the key** (matches ROADMAP intent, short); `cpu_model` (`MachineMeta`, `postprocess.rs:48-56`) stays recorded for provenance and is promotable into the key later if a same-GPU/different-CPU collision ever mis-gates the CPU-bound `cpu_reference` benches (open question). Empty device (Lavapipe-less CI) → `cpu_only__<cpu>` / `unknown_machine` fallback. `deviceUUID` (`device_uuid.rs:28`) is held in reserve for the multi-identical-GPU case (it needs `instance+pd`, not yet on `VulkanContext`).

**Layout (schema v2).** One nested file, not per-machine files (the gate reads exactly one path; the diff stays reviewable): `{ schema_version:2, machines: BTreeMap<key, { generated, git_sha, machine:MachineMeta, benchmarks:Vec<BenchEntry> }> }`. `BTreeMap` for stable key ordering (mirrors the existing BTreeMap discipline at `postprocess.rs:305`). A `MachineBlock` is the old v1 body minus the (now top-level) `schema_version` — the migration is a pure structural lift, **zero value edits**. `schema_version` bumps 1→2; the gate asserts `==2` (a v1 file is a hard error pointing at this migration); the bless path TOLERATES + auto-upgrades a v1 file (re-keys its single block) so an operator is never stuck.

**NVIDIA baseline migration (not lost — full-field).** All 15 current entries (compile_pipeline ×2, cpu_reference ×4, dispatch_gpu ×4, dispatch_gpu_amortized ×1, dispatch_gpu_q4_0 ×2, dispatch_gpu_q4km ×2) + their `low_ns/high_ns` + the machine meta move VERBATIM under `nvidia_rtx_pro_6000_blackwell_workstation_edition`. Two-step, both verified: (1) hand-edit the committed `baselines.json` into v2 (reviewable as a no-value-change diff); (2) `AXC_BLESS_BASELINES=1 cargo bench -p axc-driver` on the NVIDIA box re-derives the SAME key from the live device and re-blesses via read-modify-write. **r2 (blocker 3):** the not-lost test (AT-EB2-05) asserts the FULL per-entry tuple multiset `(group, bench, median_ns, low_ns, high_ns)` is value-exact against a frozen table — not `median_ns` only — so a `low_ns`/`high_ns` drift is also caught. It deserializes the committed file THROUGH the gate's `BaselinesV2`/`MachineBlock` structs (r2 fold — the single best writer/gate-schema-agreement check; same for AT-EB2-07).

**Bless (writer, `postprocess.rs`).** Derive the current key from the live device + cpu. Build a `MachineBlock` from this run's entries+meta. Candidate write: single-machine v2 (gitignored). Bless: **read-modify-write** — load the existing nested file, insert-or-replace ONLY `map[current_key]`, leave every other machine's block untouched, re-serialize (BTreeMap → deterministic order), write back. The read-modify-write is the load-bearing fix: a bless on machine B never clobbers machine A. **r2 (blocker 1) atomic write:** because `baselines.json` now holds EVERY machine's baselines, the bless's current non-atomic `File::create`(truncate)+`write_all` (`postprocess.rs:339-342`) is dangerous — a crash mid-write corrupts ALL machines' data. The bless MUST serialize to a TEMP file in the SAME directory then `std::fs::rename` over `baselines.json` (atomic on POSIX within one filesystem), so a crash leaves the old multi-machine file intact. **r2 (blocker 1) fail-loud on corrupt:** the read step distinguishes ABSENT (file absent → empty map; first bless) from PRESENT-BUT-UNPARSEABLE (corrupt/truncated → ERROR, refuse to bless over garbage) — NEVER a silent start-from-empty, which would drop every other machine's block (the exact clobber EB.2 prevents). Concurrent blesses race to rename (last-writer-wins): acceptable for a manual single-operator step, noted not mitigated.

**Gate (`bench_regression.rs`).** Derive the current key. `machines.get(key)`: `Some(block)` → run the EXISTING `cpu_reference` 15% gate against `block.benchmarks` (`check_regression()`, `REGRESSION_THRESHOLD_PCT=15`, `WARM_SAMPLES=11` all byte-unchanged — only the SELECTION of which block changes); `None` → **r2 (blocker 2) two-case skip:** (a) genuinely-empty file (`machines.is_empty()`, no machine ever blessed) → QUIET skip + `Ok`; (b) a real device was probed (non-empty `deviceName`) AND the file is non-empty AND the current key is absent → LOUD `WARNING` logging the current key + the sorted list of known keys + `Ok`. Case (b) surfaces a driver-rename key-drift on an EXISTING machine in CI output instead of silently passing it as "a new machine" — it is the key defense against the residual key-drift-silently-disables-the-gate risk. Honest tradeoff: the loud skip is a **CI log, not a hard gate** (CI stays green) — deliberate, because a driver rename is rare, a re-bless fixes it, and hard-failing would wrongly block a legitimate brand-new machine. **r2 (blocker 1) gate fail-loud:** an unreadable/corrupt/truncated/v1 `baselines.json` stays a HARD PANIC (`bench_regression.rs:159-178`) — the two-case skip is reached ONLY for a successfully-parsed v2 file missing the current key, NEVER for a corrupt file (else a corrupt file would silently disable the gate for ALL machines, incl. NVIDIA). No-Vulkan fallback: empty device key + exactly one machine → use that single block (preserves today's Lavapipe-less CI gate) with a logged note; empty device + >1 machine → skip-with-log (cannot disambiguate). The `machine_key()`/`sanitize()`/`BaselinesV2`/`MachineBlock` definitions + the pure `merge_blessed()` (read-modify-write, fail-loud-on-corrupt) and `select_block()` (two-case skip + fallback) live ONCE in `benches/common.rs` (already shared into the test via `#[path="../benches/common.rs"] mod common;`, `bench_regression.rs:27-28`) so writer and gate cannot drift. **r2 fold:** their PURE-FN unit tests (AT-EB2-01..04/08) live in `crates/axc-driver/tests/common_helpers.rs` (the documented C9 convention, `common.rs:5`), NOT as `#[cfg(test)]` inside `common.rs` (which is `#[path]`-included by every bench target + the gate and would compile the tests into each).

**Honesty / scope.** No codegen, no kernel, no language feature — the diff is `bench_regression.rs` + `postprocess.rs` + `common.rs` + `tests/common_helpers.rs` + `baselines.json` (+ this section). With one machine, the only live behavioral change is the new nesting; the skip-on-new-machine path is **tested but unexercised by real hardware** until EB.1 lands. The value is **forward-looking**: the harness is READY for cross-vendor so the first AMD/Intel run blesses cleanly without destroying the NVIDIA baseline and without firing a false regression. Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/EB2-architect.json` (r2; verdict PROPOSED first key). AT-EB2-01..11.

---

## 3.1.36 M3.9 — multi-subgroup warptile matmul [THE PIVOT: emitting the missing algorithm]

**The thesis, stated sharply.** SPIR-V is SPIR-V. The Vulkan driver compiles AXIOM's and llama.cpp's SPIR-V through the **same** backend, so AXIOM's residual **2.39× deficit** on the Q4_K_M matmul (M3.6 leader **42.86 TFLOPS** vs llama **102.49 TFLOPS** at the A/B shape 4096×512×14336, §3.1.22) is **not** a driver/ISA disadvantage — it is **AXIOM's compiler EMITTING A WEAKER ALGORITHM**. There is no fundamental reason AXIOM cannot emit the same kernel structure and approach the same speed. The dominant structural gap, read directly from both sources, is the **multi-subgroup warptile**.

**The two algorithms, side by side.**
- **llama `mul_mm.comp` COOPMAT path** (`mul_mm.comp:103-194` params; `:252-304` compute) is a **multi-subgroup warptile**: a workgroup of `NUM_WARPS = BLOCK_SIZE/WARP` subgroups (`mul_mm.comp:131`; BLOCK_SIZE constant_id=0 default 64, WARP=32 ⇒ 2–8 warps) cooperates on a `BM×BN` workgroup tile (BM=BN=64 default, up to 128, `:104-105`). `warp_i = gl_SubgroupID` (`:174`); each warp owns a `WM×WN` sub-tile via `warp_r = warp_i % (BM/WM)`, `warp_c = warp_i / (BM/WM)` (`:193-194`). A/B are staged into `buf_a/buf_b` **once by ALL warps cooperatively** (`:276-285`), one `barrier()` (`:287`), then each warp holds `sums[cms_per_row*cms_per_col]` coopmat accumulators (`:255-259`) and runs the K-step coopmat loop (`:293-304`) reading **its** sub-tile from shared. The staged A/B is amortized across all warps.
- **AXIOM M3.6 leader** (`examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached.axc:92` `@workgroup(32,1,1)`) = **ONE subgroup = ONE warp** = a 32×32 output tile (2×2 coopmat RB, 4 accumulators, `:129-132`,`:266-269`). Grid = (N/32, M/32, 1). AXIOM extracts ~1/Kth (K = llama's NUM_WARPS) the compute-per-workgroup and does **not** amortize the A/B stage across multiple warps. **This is the missing algorithm.**

**The M3.9 kernel (plain f32 first — isolate the warptile from the dequant).** `examples/matmul_warptile_coopmat.axc`, base = `examples/matmul_rb_coopmat.axc` (the M3.3c plain f16 matmul = the M3.6 2×2 coopmat RB minus the Q4_K_M dequant). **Config (K=4):** `@workgroup(128,1,1)` → 4 subgroups on NVIDIA wave32; `BM×BN = 64×64`; the 4 subgroups form a 2×2 grid (`sg_id = local_invocation_id(0)/subgroup_size()`, `sg_r = sg_id%2`, `sg_c = sg_id/2`), each owning a 32×32 sub-tile via the M3.6 2×2 coopmat RB (4 accumulators). A (64×16) and B (16×64) are staged into shared **once by all 128 threads** (each stages 8 A + 8 B; `ei = iter*128 + local_invocation_id(0)` covers 1024 exactly once), one `workgroup_barrier()`, then each subgroup `coopmat_load`s its A row-band (`offset = sg_r*512`) and B col-band (`offset = sg_c*32`, stride 64) and runs 4 `coopmat_mul_add`s. Grid = (N/64, M/64, 1). Each subgroup writes a **disjoint** 32×32 C region (no cross-subgroup write race).

**Pure source — NO new compiler feature (the decision).** Every ingredient exists and is proven: `@workgroup(128,1,1)` is permitted (product ≤ 1024, `validate.rs:404`; product **128 IS exactly the portable floor** `PORTABLE_MIN_WORKGROUP_INVOCATIONS` (`hir.rs:19`), so it emits **ZERO** `WorkgroupExceedsPortableFloor` warnings — the warn fires only when product **>** 128 (`validate.rs:413`, doc `validate.rs:7` "[129,1024]: WARN"); the K=2 opponent `@workgroup(64,1,1)` (64<128) is likewise clean, and M3.3d already shipped `@workgroup(64,1,1)`; a hypothetical K=8 `@workgroup(256,1,1)` variant (256>128) *would* warn but is not shipped); the subgroup-id idiom `local_invocation_id(0)/subgroup_size()` uses the M3.3d builtins (`body.rs:1421-1422`, `body.rs:2124-2153`); cooperative cross-subgroup staging is ordinary shared writes + a depth-0 `workgroup_barrier()` (M3.3d at 64 threads, here at 128); per-subgroup **runtime-offset** `coopmat_load` from shared takes a `u32` element_offset expr (`typecheck.rs:2735`; M3.7 `_db` and M3.3d already pass runtime offsets); 4 coopmat accumulators per subgroup carry via the **N-coopmat-phi** path (`body.rs:962-1006`, N-agnostic, M3.8 proved N=16) which is **per-invocation SSA** so 4 subgroups are 4 independent instances; depth-0 unconditional barriers pass `BarrierInDivergentContext` (`typecheck.rs:2465`). No new lexer token, HIR node, codegen path, or capability beyond the M3.6 set. A convenience `subgroup_id()`/`num_subgroups()` builtin is **not** needed (the div idiom suffices) and is deferred.

**The M3.3d trap, and how M3.9 avoids it.** M3.3d's negative (19.2% vs M3.3c's 24.96% at 768³, §3.1.18) was **not** "same tile, fewer workgroups" naively — M3.3d *did* use a 2× tile (32×64) — its problem was that 2× tile with K=2 subgroups **halved the workgroup count** (288 vs 576 at 768³) and added a cross-subgroup barrier, and the under-occupancy + barrier cost outweighed the staging amortization. M3.7 (double-buffer, −3%, §3.1.23) and M3.8 (4×4 RB, −34%/−39%) then **independently** confirmed via two more levers that **the kernel is OCCUPANCY-bound, not latency- or arithmetic-intensity-bound** on NVIDIA. M3.9's avoidance: choose BM×BN so the grid **still saturates the ~188 SMs** at the A/B shape. At 4096×512×14336: single-subgroup grid = 2048 workgroups (×1 warp = 2048 warps); K=4 warptile BM=BN=64 grid = 512 workgroups (×4 warps = **2048 warps — same total warps-in-flight**), but each warptile workgroup amortizes the A/B stage across 4 warps (¼ the global loads per MAC) and packs 4 warps onto an SM's warp slots. **This is the first warptile that does NOT reduce total warps-in-flight at the A/B shape.** (At 768³ the grid is 144 WGs × 4 = 576 warps, which may under-occupy — reported per-size honestly; the A/B shape is the headline.)

**Correctness — bit-exact, FROZEN, no loosening.** The plain warptile is a pure **re-partition** of the same coopmat MACs in the same per-element accumulation order, so it is **bit-identical** to single-subgroup RB at the same shape: AT-2300/2301/2302 assert `max|gpu − cpu_matmul| == 0` (small K, f16 integer-exact sum bound, M3.3c precedent); **AT-2305 (REQUIRED — not optional)** asserts cross-kernel bit-identity vs single-subgroup RB at 768³ (`n_diff == 0`) on an **asymmetric fixture** (`make_transpose_fixture_a/b`, per-row/col-varying) at the (12,12)=144-workgroup grid (>1 workgroup → multiple 64×64 tiles + the cross-subgroup cooperative staging exercised) — the load-bearing cross-subgroup-staging-race detector: a missing/mis-scoped barrier *or* a swapped/transposed sub-tile would diverge, and the asymmetric fixture ensures such a partition bug cannot pass by data symmetry. `subgroup_size()!=32` → **typed-skip** (output coverage is hard-wired to the wave32 4-subgroup partition; mirror `dispatch_matmul_tile.rs:231`). No `@equiv_fp_tol` loosening; the Q4_K_M follow-on inherits the FROZEN combined condition-aware 1e-3 untouched.

**Acceptance (HONEST, falsifiable).** PRIMARY gate (AT-2306): warptile K=4 at the A/B shape **and** 768³ is **≥ 1.3× single-subgroup RB** (768³: M3.3c 31.2 TFLOPS → gate ≥ 40.6), with `shared_memory_bytes` + workgroup-count + warps-in-flight recorded per size. **HONEST-NEGATIVE clause armed (AT-2307):** the M3.3d+M3.7+M3.8 **triad of prior negatives** — all diagnosed as the same occupancy ceiling — makes a clean ≥1.3× **this iteration UNCERTAIN**. If the warptile does not reach the gate (cross-subgroup barrier cost vs staging amortization; 4-warp register co-residency cutting achieved occupancy; shared-bank conflicts on the 64-wide `b_tile`; or simply the occupancy ceiling again), that is **reported with per-size numbers + a diagnosis of the residual bottleneck**, never hidden, never papered by loosening the gate — and the durable deliverable becomes "AXIOM **expresses** the multi-subgroup warptile pure-source + the diagnosis pointing at vectorized loads (llama's `LOAD_VEC_A/B`, `mul_mm.comp:196-202`) and/or a `SHMEM_STRIDE` bank pad (`mul_mm.comp:123-125`) as the next lever (a possible M3.10)." **No llama-parity claim** until measured — M3.9 is gap-NARROWING. Single-subgroup RB and the M3.6 Q4_K_M leader remain the production leaders unless the gate is beaten. The **Q4_K_M fusion** (drop the M3.6 dequant front-end onto the warptile's cooperative A staging) is an explicit **follow-on**, not in M3.9 scope — M3.9 ships the plain warptile to isolate the variable.

**Implementation status (Coder, M3.9).** Both kernels written **PURE-SOURCE** (no compiler change): `examples/matmul_warptile_coopmat.axc` (K=4, `@workgroup(128,1,1)`, 4 subgroups, 64×64 tile, 2×2 sub-tile grid via the M3.6 2×2 coopmat RB) + `examples/matmul_warptile_coopmat_2sg.axc` (K=2 opponent, `@workgroup(64,1,1)`, 32×64 tile). **NR fail-fast PASSED**: both compile via `compile_source_with_assignments`, pass `spirv-val` (Vulkan 1.1), and the emitted **capability set is byte-identical to `matmul_rb_coopmat.axc`** (no new capability beyond the M3.6 set `{Shader, VulkanMemoryModel, CooperativeMatrixKHR, GroupNonUniform, Float16, …}`). **AT-2304** (CI, no-GPU) asserts the cap-set equality + **ZERO `WorkgroupExceedsPortableFloor` warnings** for both kernels (`@workgroup(128)` product==128 IS the portable floor → clean; `@workgroup(64)`<128 → clean), tolerating the base kernel's pre-existing `UnusedStrategyHole` warns. `shared_memory_bytes` = 4096 (K=4: a_tile 1024 + b_tile 1024 f16) / 3072 (K=2). The depth-0 RAW barrier is kept BEFORE the first `coopmat_load` (static RAW analyzer); the WAR barrier at the K-loop bottom; both `workgroup_barrier()` → Workgroup-scope `OpControlBarrier` syncing all 4 subgroups. AT-2300/2301/2302/2303 (bit-exact vs CPU oracle) + **AT-2305 (REQUIRED cross-subgroup-race detector — BIT-IDENTITY vs single-subgroup RB on an ASYMMETRIC tiled `make_transpose_fixture_a/b` at 768³=144 workgroups)** are `#[ignore]`-gated for the orchestrator's NVIDIA run. Bench `resident_matmul_warptile.rs` (AT-2306/2307) measures K=4 vs K=2 vs single-subgroup RB at 256/512/768/1024 + the A/B shape, recording per-size TFLOPS + `shared_memory_bytes` + workgroup-count + warps-in-flight + the honest ratio (gate evaluated, never asserted). Script flag `--warptile` runs the plain-warptile resident gate (short-circuits the Q4_K_M/llama path; the fusion is the M3.10 follow-on).

**MEASURED RESULT (NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell — HONEST-NEGATIVE, AT-2307 armed).** CORRECTNESS first (gates the perf number): AT-2300/2301/2302 K=4 bit-exact vs CPU oracle (`max_diff=0` at K=32/48), AT-2303 K=2 bit-exact, and **AT-2305 K=4 BIT-IDENTICAL to single-subgroup RB (`n_diff=0/589824`) on the ASYMMETRIC tiled `make_transpose_fixture_a/b` at 768³=144 workgroups** — the cross-subgroup cooperative staging + the 2×2 sub-tile partition + the cross-subgroup barriers are PROVEN correct (a swapped sub-tile or a missing/mis-scoped barrier would break bit-identity, and on NVIDIA a missing cross-subgroup barrier manifests). PERF (MIN-of-10, GpuTimestamp): the warptile K=4 is **NOT** a win — it is **0.86×–0.99× single-subgroup RB at every size**, MISSING the ≥1.3× gate: **A/B (4096×512×14336) K=4 ≈ 72.5 TFLOPS vs single-subgroup RB ≈ 75.8 = 0.955×**; **768³ K=4 ≈ 28.9 vs 29.5 = 0.98×**; 1024³ 45.3/52.3 = 0.87×; 512³ 12.0/13.1 = 0.91×; 256³ 2.56/2.75 = 0.93× (the K=2 opponent is uniformly slower still, ~0.85–0.93×, re-confirming the M3.3d structure). **DIAGNOSIS → M3.10.** The M3.3d-trap avoidance WORKED at the warp level (warptile K=4 keeps **warps-in-flight == single-subgroup**: A/B both = 2048; 768³ both = 576) and staging is genuinely amortized 4× (shared 4096 B/WG = 1 KB/warp, HALF single-subgroup's per-warp), yet the warptile is marginally **slower**. So the residual cost is **NOT** staging-load volume and **NOT** warp-count occupancy (both equalized) — it is the **cross-subgroup `workgroup_barrier` synchronization cost + the 4-warp-per-WG register/shared CO-RESIDENCY** (4× the registers + 2× the shared per workgroup ⇒ fewer *workgroups* resident per SM at the same warp count, the M3.8-class mechanism at WG granularity) plus the **un-vectorized scalar staging instruction count** (8 scalar A + 8 scalar B loads/thread/k_block). The warps-equal-but-slower signature points the next lever at **llama's vectorized loads (`LOAD_VEC_A/B`, `mul_mm.comp:196-202`, 2–4 f16/thread/iter — halves the staging instruction count) + a `SHMEM_STRIDE` bank pad (`mul_mm.comp:123-125`)** — proposed as **M3.10**, isolated deliberately out of M3.9. **Durable M3.9 deliverable:** AXIOM **expresses the full multi-subgroup warptile PURE-SOURCE** (no compiler change — 4 subgroups, cooperative cross-subgroup staging, depth-0 workgroup barriers, runtime-offset per-subgroup coopmat loads, 4×4=16 coopmat accumulators across 4 N-phi instances), **bit-identical-correct**, + the diagnosis. Single-subgroup RB and the M3.6 Q4_K_M leader REMAIN the production leaders; the gate was NOT loosened to claim a win. No llama-parity claim. (Artifact: `.pipeline/benchmarks/m34/ab_results_warptile.json` + `warptile_bench.txt`.)*

*(Status: **PROPOSED (Architect) — r2** after dual design review (Pessimistic APPROVE; Optimistic one factual-test-spec blocker, now fixed). **r1→r2:** (1) **AT-2304 blocker fixed** — r1 wrongly claimed `@workgroup(128,1,1)` triggers a `WorkgroupExceedsPortableFloor` warn; in fact `PORTABLE_MIN_WORKGROUP_INVOCATIONS==128` (`hir.rs:19`) and the warn fires only at product **>** 128 (`validate.rs:413`), so 128 is CLEAN. AT-2304 now asserts **ZERO** `WorkgroupExceedsPortableFloor` warns (for both `@workgroup(128)` and the `@workgroup(64)` opponent), tolerating the base kernel's pre-existing `UnusedStrategyHole` warns (assert *absence* of the portable-floor variant, not warning-free overall). (2) **AT-2305 promoted optional→REQUIRED** with an asymmetric fixture (`make_transpose_fixture_a/b`) at the 768³ (12,12)=144-workgroup shape (>1 workgroup; the load-bearing cross-subgroup-barrier/sub-tile-swap detector that can't pass by symmetry). (3) staging iter-count noted **K-independent** (a_iters=b_iters=8). (4) pessimistic advisories confirmed (preserve the post-staging RAW barrier placement for the static analyzer; occupancy/shared-budget sound). All else unchanged. Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/M39-architect.json` (verdict PROPOSED first key, revision r2). PURE SOURCE — no new builtin/codegen/capability; new artifacts: `examples/matmul_warptile_coopmat.axc` (K=4), `examples/matmul_warptile_coopmat_2sg.axc` (K=2 A/B opponent), `crates/axc-driver/benches/resident_matmul_warptile.rs`, `crates/axc-driver/tests/dispatch_matmul_warptile.rs`, `--warptile` in `scripts/m34_llamacpp_ab.sh`, `compile_shared_examples.rs` AT-2304. Honest faster-than-single-subgroup gate (≥1.3× @ 768³/A-B) + armed honest-negative clause (the M3.3d/M3.7/M3.8 occupancy-ceiling triad). FROZEN tol untouched, real GPU correctness, spirv-val mandatory. AT-2300..AT-2308.)*

## 3.1.37 M3.10 — the memory-subsystem levers: shared-memory bank padding (M3.10a, pure source) + a vec-load builtin (M3.10b, scoped/conditional)

**The campaign state, stated sharply.** SPIR-V is SPIR-V (§3.1.36): AXIOM's M3.6 Q4_K_M leader (**42.86 TFLOPS**) is **2.39× behind** llama.cpp (**102.49 TFLOPS**) at the A/B shape 4096×512×14336 because AXIOM emits a **weaker algorithm**, not weaker SPIR-V. The four prior "fix the compiler" experiments isolated the bottleneck by *elimination*: **M3.7** (double-buffering, 0.97×) ruled out global-load **latency**; **M3.8** (4×4/4×2 register tiles, 0.66×/0.61×) ruled out **arithmetic-intensity**; **M3.3d** (multi-subgroup, 0.77×) and **M3.9** (warptile K=4, 0.955× — *warps-in-flight AND staging volume equalized*, yet still marginally slower) ruled out **warp-count occupancy** and **staging-load volume**. The triad is consistent: the M3.6 2×2 single-subgroup kernel is at the occupancy/compute **sweet spot**, and the residual cost lives in the **memory subsystem** — exactly the two classic post-tiling GEMM levers llama.cpp uses and AXIOM does not: **(1) un-vectorized scalar global→shared staging** (AXIOM stages one f16/f32 per `OpLoad`; llama's `LOAD_VEC_A/B`, `mul_mm.comp:196-202`, moves vec2/vec4/u8vec4 per transaction) and **(2) shared-memory bank conflicts** (AXIOM's shared tiles are un-padded; llama's `SHMEM_STRIDE`, `mul_mm.comp:123-125`, pads each shared row so consecutive rows hit different banks). M3.10 attacks both — **sequenced to isolate each variable cleanly, each with its own falsifiable gate and an armed honest-negative** (the M3.7/M3.8/M3.9 discipline).

**The codegen-feasibility verdict (the milestone's central technical question, read from the source).** I read the SSBO emission (`crates/axc-codegen/src/buffers.rs:99-134`), the coopmat load/store emission (`crates/axc-codegen/src/coopmat.rs:206-345`), the shared read/write emission (`crates/axc-codegen/src/shared.rs:160-209`), the scalar byte-load builtins (`crates/axc-codegen/src/q4_0.rs`), and the addressing model (`crates/axc-codegen/src/emit.rs:159` — **`OpMemoryModel Logical Vulkan`**, NO PhysicalStorageBuffer / buffer_device_address anywhere). Two facts decide the scope:

  1. **Bank padding is PURE SOURCE — expressible TODAY, no codegen change.** `coopmat_load(buf_or_shared, element_offset, stride)` already takes the **stride as a runtime `u32`** (`coopmat.rs:269/323` — the matmul kernels pass `16u32`/`32u32`/`64u32` row strides as ordinary expressions; M3.9's per-subgroup loads pass *runtime* strides). The scalar shared write/read (`shared.rs:177/202`) is a **single-index `OpAccessChain` with an arbitrary `u32` index** — `tile[row*(cols+PAD) + col]` is just integer arithmetic. So a bank-conflict-avoiding padded shared tile is built entirely in source: allocate `shared[f16, rows*(cols+PAD)]`, write each staged element at the padded index `r*(cols+PAD)+c`, and pass `cols+PAD` as the coopmat_load `stride`. **NOTHING in the index math or the coopmat_load stride blocks it.** ⇒ **M3.10a is a pure-source variant of the M3.6 leader (and the plain-f32 RB), isolating the bank-conflict variable. DO IT FIRST (cheap, fail-fast, zero codegen risk).**

  2. **Vectorized loads are NOT expressible in pure source AND a "minimal builtin" hits a hard SPIR-V-layout wall under Logical addressing.** The SSBO is `OpTypeStruct { OpTypeRuntimeArray %scalar_T }` with `ArrayStride = sizeof(scalar)` (`buffers.rs:108/130`). A genuine single-transaction vec4 `OpLoad` requires reaching a **vector-typed lvalue**. Under **`AddressingModel::Logical`** (confirmed, `emit.rs:159`) you **CANNOT** `OpBitcast` a `ptr-to-scalar` into a `ptr-to-vec4` and load through it — Logical addressing forbids pointer-type bitcasts; the access-chain element type is fixed by the runtime array's *declared* element type. Therefore a real vec4 load needs the **runtime-array element type to BE the vector** (`OpTypeRuntimeArray %v4f16`, `ArrayStride=8`, a vec-typed struct member) — i.e. a **second, vec-typed SSBO interface/view of the same buffer with a DIFFERENT `ArrayStride` decoration**. That is a **StorageBuffer-layout/binding change**, not a "smallest builtin." A `load_vec4(buf, base_idx) -> <4×scalar>` builtin that scatters to shared would still need *some* vec-typed access; the only honest realizations are (a) declare a parallel vec4-typed runtime-array view (layout change + a return value that is a vector — but AXIOM has **NO vector types in the type system at all**: `ty.rs` is scalar | buffer | matrix | shared | subgroup, no `vec*`, confirmed — so the builtin's *return type* has no home and the scatter-to-shared must happen *inside* the builtin's codegen), or (b) emit 4 scalar `OpLoad`s and hope the driver coalesces them (which is **exactly what AXIOM does today** — "scalar loads dressed up," the very thing the AT falsifier must reject). **HONEST FLAG: a real vec-load is NOT a minimal in-keeping builtin; it requires either first-class vector types (a large language lift, explicitly OUT of scope) or a vec-typed SSBO-view + a self-contained scatter-to-shared codegen builtin (a genuine new codegen + a layout decoration the existing `buffers.rs` path does not emit).** This is the milestone's load-bearing finding.

**SCOPE DECISION — M3.10a (ship) + M3.10b (feasibility spike, conditional/deferred).** Because (2) shows vec-loads need a real codegen+layout lift while (1) shows bank-padding is free, M3.10 is **scoped to the pure-source bank-padding milestone (M3.10a) as the shippable deliverable**, with **M3.10b (the vec-load builtin) gated behind M3.10a's signal and explicitly written as a feasibility spike that may be DEFERRED**. Rationale, in the campaign's own discipline: (i) a pure-source bank-padding win that beats the leader is **itself a valid milestone** and the cheapest possible probe of the memory-subsystem hypothesis; (ii) if bank-padding shows nothing, the residual is likely *not* shared-bank-conflict-bound either, which **down-weights** the vec-load lift before paying for it (fail fast); (iii) the vec-load builtin's true cost (vec-typed SSBO view + Logical-addressing-safe scatter codegen + the no-vector-types-in-the-language constraint) warrants its **own** architect cycle once M3.10a quantifies how much of the 2.39× is bank-conflict vs staging-instruction-count.

**BASE-KERNEL SEQUENCE (fail-fast, isolate vectorization+padding from the Q4_K_M dequant).** Apply each lever to the **plain-f32 single-subgroup RB leader** (`examples/matmul_rb_coopmat.axc`) **FIRST** — it is the production single-subgroup path minus the Q4_K_M dequant, so a win/loss there isolates the *matmul-core* memory effect from the dequant front-end. **THEN**, only if the plain-f32 padded variant shows a win, port the pad to the **M3.6 Q4_K_M cached leader** (`examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached.axc`, the real llama fight). If plain-f32 shows nothing, the Q4_K_M port is a likely-negative too and is reported as such without spending the cycle.

**M3.10a MECHANISM — PURE SOURCE, padded shared tiles (NO compiler change).** New kernels `examples/matmul_rb_coopmat_pad.axc` (plain-f32, base = `matmul_rb_coopmat.axc`) and, conditional on the plain-f32 win, `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached_pad.axc` (base = M3.6 leader). The ONLY change vs the base: the A and/or B shared tiles gain a per-row pad. For the 2×2 RB block the A tile is 32 rows × 16 cols (`tile_k=16`); padding it to stride `16+PAD` makes it `shared[f16, 32*(16+PAD)]`; the staging write index becomes `a_row*(16+PAD) + a_col` and the two A coopmat_loads pass **stride `16+PAD`** at offsets `0` and `16*(16+PAD)` (was `256`). The B tile is 16 rows × 32 cols; padding to `32+PAD_B` makes it `shared[f16, 16*(32+PAD_B)]`, the B write index `b_row*(32+PAD_B)+b_col`, and the two B coopmat_loads pass **stride `32+PAD_B`** at offsets `0` and `16`. **`PAD`/`PAD_B` is a `@strategy` hole** (e.g. `pad: ?[1,2,4,8]`) so the autotuner sweeps the bank-conflict-avoiding stride; the canonical ship value (e.g. f16 → 8 banks/16B → a +8-elem pad to break the 16-wide stride's 2-way conflict) is pinned after the sweep, exactly as M3.6/M3.8 pinned their holes. Barriers, the single-level OpPhi K-carry, the 4 mul_adds, coopmat_store, the dequant arithmetic (Q4_K_M variant) are **VERBATIM** the base. **NO new builtin/codegen/capability/typecheck change** (larger `shared[f16,N]` within `MAX_SHARED_ELEMS=65536`; runtime coopmat_load stride already accepted `typecheck.rs:2777`; padded write index is ordinary `u32` arithmetic). The M3.6-class traps are structurally absent (no nested coopmat carry, no conditional coopmat reassign, no barrier-in-if).

**M3.10a CORRECTNESS — bit-identical, FROZEN 1e-3 untouched.** Padding changes only the shared **address layout**, never the arithmetic, the coopmat tile order, or the accumulation order: the same 16×16 tiles are loaded from the same logical (row,col) cells (now at padded offsets) and fed to the same `coopmat_mul_add`s in the same order. So each output element is **BIT-IDENTICAL** to the base. The load-bearing gate is the cross-kernel bit-identity anchor (`max|y_pad − y_base|.to_bits() == 0`): plain-f32 vs `matmul_rb_coopmat.axc`, and (Q4_K_M variant) vs the M3.6 leader. The COMBINED condition-aware metric `|gpu−ref|/max(|ref|,Σ|wₖxₖ|) ≤` **FROZEN 1e-3** (Q4_K_M, K=256/512/14336 vs `common_q4km_f32ref.rs`) is the floor authority and **NEVER loosens** (a wrong-but-padded read — e.g. a stride/offset that mis-addresses the padded cells — would break bit-identity AND the combined metric; the asymmetric fixture below ensures a mis-stride cannot pass by symmetry). spirv-val CANNOT catch a mis-strided shared read — the real-GPU bit-identity gate is mandatory (anti-pattern #9).

**M3.10a ACCEPTANCE (HONEST, falsifiable, measured NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000).** PRIMARY exit gate: the plain-f32 padded kernel at 768³ (and the A/B shape for the Q4_K_M variant if it advances) is **≥ 1.15× the corresponding base** (plain-f32 768³ vs `matmul_rb_coopmat.axc`; Q4_K_M A/B ≥ 1.15× M3.6's 42.86 = ≥ 49.3 TFLOPS) AND bit-identical AND (Q4_K_M) combined ≤ 1e-3. **ARMED HONEST-NEGATIVE:** the M3.7/M3.8/M3.9 triad makes a clean win UNCERTAIN. If padding does not reach 1.15× — because the M3.6 tile is NOT bank-conflict-bound (16-wide f16 coopmat loads may already be conflict-free on Blackwell), OR the +pad shared footprint drops occupancy and net-regresses (the M3.7/M3.3d mechanism), OR the win is real but < 1.15× — that is **REPORTED with the measured ratio + a per-size `shared_memory_bytes`/occupancy/bank diagnosis**, the gate is **NOT loosened**, and M3.6/the plain RB remain the production leaders. A measured sub-gate win is still recorded honestly (it informs the vec-load decision).

**M3.10b (CONDITIONAL — the vec-load builtin feasibility spike, may DEFER).** Gated behind M3.10a's signal. IF M3.10a shows the kernel IS memory-subsystem-sensitive (padding helps, even sub-gate), THEN spike a `load_vec4_to_shared(src_buf, src_base_idx, dst_shared, dst_base_idx)` builtin (NOT a value-returning `load_vec4` — AXIOM has no vector types to return into; the builtin must be a **self-contained 4-element global→shared scatter** so the vector lives only inside its codegen): emit a vec4-typed SSBO **view** (`OpTypeRuntimeArray %v4f16` with `ArrayStride=8` on a second member/binding, or a second SSBO arg aliasing the buffer) + one vec `OpLoad` + 4 `OpCompositeExtract` + 4 shared `OpStore`. The HIR-node→typecheck→codegen path mirrors the proven `ptr_read_u16_zext`/`f32_to_f16` builtin template (`q4_0.rs` + `crates/axc-codegen/src/q4_0.rs`). **Feasibility deliverable (the spike's actual output):** a compile + spirv-val PASS/FAIL verdict on the vec4-typed-SSBO-view-under-Logical-addressing approach, the exact capability/extension delta (assert whether it stays in the M3.6 cap set — vector loads are core SPIR-V, no new capability *expected*, but the layout-decoration delta and any `StorageBuffer16BitAccess` vec interaction MUST be measured, not assumed), and a falsifier that the **emitted SPIR-V contains a vector-typed `OpLoad`** (Op == OpLoad, result type Op == OpTypeVector, component count 4), NOT 4 scalar loads dressed up. If the vec-typed-view approach fails spirv-val or needs first-class vector types throughout, **M3.10b is DEFERRED with the documented blocker** and M3.10 ships as M3.10a-only — an honest, valid milestone.

**Acceptance discipline (both sub-features).** Per-feature: compile + spirv-val clean; cap-set delta asserted (padding: byte-identical to base; vec-load: measured, no-new-capability asserted or the delta documented); bit-identical (plain) / combined-VALID (Q4_K_M) vs the relevant leader on the **asymmetric fixture** (`make_transpose_fixture_a/b` — the load-bearing mis-stride detector); the isolated A/B TFLOPS vs the leader (the gate, printed + checked, NEVER loosened); cross-kernel no-regression for the existing leaders; the FROZEN-1e-3-untouched assertion; and (vec-load) the emitted-vector-`OpLoad` falsifier. Real NVIDIA bit-identity/combined-VALID is the mandatory gate; spirv-val + the CI compile anchor are necessary but NOT a correctness proof.

**Reused infrastructure.** `common_q4km_f32ref.rs` (oracle), the resident bench methodology (`resident_matmul_rb.rs` → `_pad`; `resident_q4km_matmul_rb_f32acc_cached.rs` → `_pad`), `scripts/m34_llamacpp_ab.sh` (`--pad` flag, `ab_results_*_pad.json`), `compile_shared_examples.rs` (the CI compile/cap/shared-bytes anchor), and the dispatch + bit-identity equiv scaffolding (cloned). New artifacts: `examples/matmul_rb_coopmat_pad.axc` (plain-f32, headline), `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached_pad.axc` (Q4_K_M, conditional on the plain-f32 win). AT-2400..AT-2412.

**MEASURED OUTCOME — ⚠️ HONEST-NEGATIVE, MERGED as a documented experiment (NOT a leader), 2026-06-10.** M3.10a shipped pure-source (the codegen/hir/parser/lexer/typecheck diff is EMPTY — verified by QA + both code reviewers); both padded kernels `examples/matmul_rb_coopmat_pad.axc` (plain-f32) + `examples/q4km_matmul_rb_coopmat_f32acc_cached_pad.axc` (Q4_K_M) compile + spirv-val clean with **byte-identical capability sets** to their bases (no new capability), and are **BIT-IDENTICAL / combined-VALID** to the leaders (plain-f32 AT-2400 `n_diff=0/589824` on the asymmetric 768³ fixture; Q4_K_M AT-2402 combined **4.13e-7 / 4.67e-7 / 1.54e-6** at K=256/512/14336 ≤ FROZEN 1e-3, AT-2403 `n_diff=0` vs the M3.6 leader). The pessimistic code review confirmed at the EMITTED-SPIR-V level that the padding is correct: the `OpTypeCooperativeMatrixKHR` stays **16×16** while only the **Stride** operand carries the pad (A loads `stride=24`, offsets `0`/`384=16·24`; B loads `stride=40`, offsets `0`/**`16`** — the B second-tile offset correctly does NOT scale with the row pad), and the scalar write index `r*(cols+PAD)+c` matches the padded read stride. **PERF — the ≥1.15× gate is MISSED at every PAD, NOT loosened:** plain-f32 768³ base ≈ **31.6 TFLOPS**; **PAD1=0.79–0.81×, PAD2=0.86–0.87×, PAD4=0.86–0.87×, PAD8=0.87–0.89× (best)** — every pad **REGRESSES** (QA independently reproduced the direction + magnitude). **DIAGNOSIS — the matmul-core is NOT shared-bank-conflict-bound on Blackwell:** the regression is consistent with OQ-1 (the coopmat tile-load path already swizzles the 16-wide f16 conflict away, so padding buys no conflict reduction) compounded by the M3.7/M3.3d occupancy mechanism (the +pad shared footprint + non-power-of-2 stride cost more than any conflict saved). **This RULES OUT shared-bank-conflicts as the residual bottleneck** — combined with M3.7 (not latency-bound), M3.8 (not arithmetic-intensity-bound), and M3.9/M3.3d (not warp-occupancy / staging-volume-bound), the "fix the compiler" campaign has now eliminated **every pure-source lever**. The one lever still standing is **vectorized-load instruction-count (M3.10b)** — reducing the *number* of staging `OpLoad`s per transaction (distinct from bank conflicts and from staging volume), which genuinely requires the codegen+layout feature AXIOM lacks (a vec-typed SSBO view under Logical addressing + the no-vector-types constraint). **M3.10b = NO-GO-DEFERRED** (not built; the layout-lift reasoning above stands; a future vec view must alias only the f16 *staging* buffers, never the frozen Q4_K_M packed-q 144-byte layout that M4.1 zero-copy depends on). The unpadded RB + the M3.6 Q4_K_M leader remain production. Pipeline: Architect → both design reviewers APPROVE → Coder → QA PASS → both code reviewers APPROVE (optimistic's one fix = this realized-outcome record). 994 tests green. AT-2400..AT-2412. ⇒ next = **M3.10b as a dedicated codegen milestone** (vec-typed SSBO view + `load_vec4_to_shared` scatter builtin) — the last-standing hypothesis for the 2.39× gap.*

*(Original design status: **PROPOSED (Architect)**. Scope: M3.10a = pure-source shared-memory bank padding (padded shared tile stride + padded coopmat_load stride + padded write index — `coopmat_load` already takes a runtime stride `coopmat.rs:269/323`, shared access is single-index runtime `u32` `shared.rs:177/202`, NO codegen change), plain-f32 RB first then conditionally the M3.6 Q4_K_M leader. M3.10b = a CONDITIONAL vec-load feasibility spike (`load_vec4_to_shared` self-contained scatter builtin), gated behind M3.10a's signal and DEFERRABLE — vec-loads are NOT pure-source (Logical addressing forbids ptr-to-vec bitcast `emit.rs:159`; needs a vec-typed SSBO view = a layout change + the no-vector-types-in-the-language constraint), an honest codegen+layout lift, not a minimal builtin. Per-feature ≥1.15× gate vs the relevant leader + armed honest-negative (the M3.7/M3.8/M3.9/M3.3d occupancy/memory triad makes a clean win UNCERTAIN — measured ratio + bank/occupancy diagnosis reported either way, gate never loosened). Bit-identical (plain) / combined-VALID (Q4_K_M) vs the leader on the asymmetric fixture; FROZEN 1e-3 untouched; real-GPU correctness mandatory; spirv-val mandatory; vec-load AT carries an emitted-vector-`OpLoad` falsifier. Design spec → `.pipeline/runs/M310-architect.json` (verdict PROPOSED first key). AT-2400..AT-2412.)*

### 3.1 Types

```
Primitives:     i8 i16 i32 i64 u8 u16 u32 u64 f16 bf16 f32 f64 bool
Vectors:        vec2..vec4 (f32), dvec2..dvec4 (f64), ivec2..ivec4 (i32), uvec2..uvec4 (u32)
Buffers:        buffer[T]              // SSBO on Vulkan, cl_mem on OpenCL
                readonly_buffer[T]     // readonly decoration
                writeonly_buffer[T]    // writeonly decoration
Images:         image2d[T] image3d[T]  // opaque image handles
Shared:         shared[T, N]           // workgroup-local memory (SLM)
Matrices:       matrix[T, M, N]        // cooperative_matrix
Subgroup:       subgroup[T]            // subgroup-uniform values
```

### 3.2 GPU-specific annotations (the core value add)

| Annotation | Meaning | Lowering |
|---|---|---|
| `@kernel` | Entry point (compute shader) | `OpEntryPoint GLCompute` + `OpExecutionMode` |
| `@workgroup(X, Y, Z)` | Workgroup dimensions | `LocalSize` execution mode |
| `@subgroup_uniform` | Value invariant across subgroup | `SubgroupUniform` decoration |
| `@uniform_branch` | Control flow uniform across subgroup | Guides structurizer |
| `@divergence_free` | No divergence in this region | Verified + unlocks shuffles |
| `@coalesced(stride=1)` | Adjacent threads access adjacent elements | Verified by access-pattern analysis |
| `@shared_tile(M, N, pad=K)` | SLM tile with bank-conflict padding | Emits `shared[T, M*(N+K)]` |
| `@no_bank_conflict` | Asserted; verified by swizzle/stride analysis | Compile-time check |
| `@cooperative_matrix(M, N, K, A_type, B_type, C_type)` | Tensor-core-style tile op | `OpCooperativeMatrixLoadKHR` / `MulAddKHR` |
| `@occupancy(min=0.5)` | Minimum target occupancy | Register-pressure estimate; rejects if violated |
| `@max_registers(N)` | Register budget | Informs codegen + spill heuristics |
| `@async_copy` | Overlap memory + compute | `cp.async` / equivalent where available |
| `@reduction(op)` | Parallel reduction over subgroup/workgroup | `SubgroupReduce` / tree reduction |
| `@barrier(scope)` | Explicit barrier with scope | `OpControlBarrier` |
| `@equiv_fp_tol(eps)` | FP tolerance for rewrite verification | Correctness oracle |
| `@strategy { tile_m: ?, tile_n: ?, stages: ? }` | LLM autotune hole | Resolved by `axc optimize` |
| `@strict` | Require `@intent` + `@complexity` + pre/post on every kernel | Compile error if missing |
| `@precondition(expr)` / `@postcondition(expr)` | Runtime-checkable contract | Debug-mode assertion |
| `@target(vendor, caps)` | Target capability set | Guards codegen paths |

### 3.3 SPIR-V extensions in scope

**Portable baseline (must work on NVIDIA/AMD/Intel):**
- `SPV_KHR_shader_subgroup*` (ballot, vote, shuffle, reduce)
- `SPV_KHR_cooperative_matrix` (tensor-core portable access, promoted in Vulkan 1.3.300)
- `SPV_KHR_float_controls`
- `SPV_KHR_16bit_storage` / `SPV_KHR_8bit_storage`
- `SPV_KHR_integer_dot_product`
- `SPV_EXT_shader_atomic_float_add`

**Vendor fast paths (path-split, not required):**
- `SPV_NV_cooperative_matrix2` (workgroup-scope matrices, per-element ops) -- NVIDIA only
- `SPV_INTEL_*` -- Intel DPC++ compute

---

## 4. Phased plan

### Phase M0 -- Bootstrap (week 0-2)

- Cargo workspace with 7 empty crates
- Lexer port from AXIOM with GPU keyword additions
- `axc lex` CLI emitting tokens
- CI on GitHub Actions (test + build)
- `.pipeline/` copied and adapted (this document produced)
- First kernel compiles (empty kernel with `@workgroup(64,1,1)`) → SPIR-V → spirv-val clean

**Exit gate M0:** `axc compile empty_kernel.axc -o empty.spv && spirv-val empty.spv` succeeds on Linux CI.

**M0 addendum:** Correctness for M0 is syntactic (spirv-val only). GPU dispatch + Lavapipe fallback + equivalence checks all begin at M1 per exit gate. Anti-pattern #9 (no feature without a GPU test) is formally relaxed for M0; re-armed from M1 onward.

### Phase M1 -- Minimum viable SPIR-V backend (month 1-3)

- Parser + HIR + SPIR-V codegen path for: scalar ops, buffers, workgroup dispatch, barriers, subgroup ops
- `axc run` dispatcher on Vulkan via `ash`
- Validator integration (`spirv-val`, `spirv-opt`)
- Saxpy + vector add + parallel reduction benchmarks running on NVIDIA + AMD + Intel + Lavapipe

**Exit gate M1:** 3-vendor execution of 5 elementwise kernels, correctness verified vs CPU reference.

### Phase M2 -- Cooperative matrix + llama.cpp Q4_K_M matmul (month 3-9)

- `@cooperative_matrix` lowering to `SPV_KHR_cooperative_matrix`
- `@strategy` hole infrastructure + LLM autotuner
- MCP server port from AXIOM
- llama.cpp Q4_K_M dequant + matmul reference kernel
- Bench harness producing `tok/s` comparison vs llama.cpp current Vulkan baseline

**Exit gate M2:** On RTX 4090, within 5% of hand-tuned llama.cpp Vulkan Q4_K_M. On AMD APU or Intel Arc, beat current Vulkan by ≥25%. One annotated source, three targets.

### Phase M3 -- FlashAttention + KernelBench-Vulkan submission (month 9-15)

- Attention kernel (FA2 shape, then FA3 where extensions allow)
- First public SPIR-V KernelBench submission
- `@equiv_fp_tol` correctness oracle (Sakana-proof verification)

**Exit gate M3:** ≥80% of cuBLAS+FA3 on H100 via Vulkan compute; ≥90% of rocBLAS on MI300X. Public KernelBench leaderboard entry.

### Phase M4 -- PyTorch custom-op frontend + adoption (month 15-24)

- `torch.compile` backend or `torch.library` custom-op integration
- Upstream PR to llama.cpp (or candle/MLX) replacing at least one hot kernel with AXIOM-Compute output

**Exit gate M4:** External adoption by ≥1 production inference framework.

---

## 5. Kill criteria (pre-registered)

Stop the project if, at the stated gate:

- **M1 slip (>6 mo):** cannot produce a correctness-verified SPIR-V kernel on 3 vendors. Means the validator/lowering path is structurally broken.
- **M2 slip (>12 mo):** cannot match llama.cpp Vulkan Q4_K_M within 15% on *any* vendor. Means annotation-to-SPIR-V lowering adds no value above handwritten GLSL.
- **M3 slip (>18 mo):** FA variant cannot clear 50% of cuBLAS+FA3 on any GPU. Means LLMs + annotations cannot close the handwritten-vendor-kernel gap -- project thesis is refuted.
- **Ecosystem preempt:** if Slang grows an equivalent annotation sublanguage, or Triton-Vulkan ships and hits M2 targets first, pivot to contribute there instead of competing.

---

## 6. Development workflow

7-agent pipeline inherited from AXIOM (see `.pipeline/PIPELINE.md`):

1. **Architect** (Opus) -- designs spec + plan
2. **Optimistic Design Reviewer** (Sonnet) -- validates feasibility + completeness
3. **Pessimistic Design Reviewer** (Opus) -- adversarial review
4. **Coder** (Sonnet) -- implements agreed plan
5. **QA** (Sonnet) -- verifies test conformance, not-trust-always-verify
6. **Optimistic Code Reviewer** (Sonnet) -- spec compliance + quality
7. **Pessimistic Code Reviewer** (Opus) -- UB, races, correctness

**Verdict enforcement:** every reviewer agent emits `{"verdict": "APPROVE|REJECT|NEEDS_REVISION"}` as the FIRST key in a JSON object. Supervisor parses this and gates the next pipeline phase. Memory-tracked rule: agents lie about their own work, so independent adversarial verification is mandatory.

**Memory limits:** test executables must not exceed 50 GB (from prior incident with a 336 GB crash).

---

## 7. Open questions (M0 -> architect resolves)

1. OpenCL SPIR-V flavor vs Vulkan SPIR-V flavor -- emit both, or OpenCL first?
2. Direct `rspirv` emission vs going through LLVM SPIR-V backend -- which for M1?
3. Naming: `.axc` for source files (for AXIOM-Compute) or `.axm` reused? Current doc assumes `.axc`.
4. Runtime dispatch: start with Kompute (Vulkan-only) or `ash` (more control)? Current doc assumes `ash`.
5. How much should we inherit from `axiom` as a git subtree vs re-implement? Prefer crate-level reuse where APIs are stable.
6. Where is the line between `axc-optimize` (autotuner) and `axc-driver` (MCP server)? Keep them separate, match AXIOM layout.

### Integer division undefined behavior

SPIR-V `OpSDiv` and `OpSRem` with `INT_MIN / -1` (e.g., `i32::MIN / -1`) are UNDEFINED
BEHAVIOR per SPIR-V unified spec §3.32.14. AXIOM-Compute does NOT emit runtime checks
for this case. Programs that rely on well-defined behavior for this specific input
must guard it manually at the source level, e.g.:

    let x: i32 = if a == -2147483648 && b == -1 { -2147483648 } else { a / b };

The same UB applies to unsigned integer division by zero and signed remainder by zero:
both are undefined. Compile-time constant-folded cases (both operands literals that
trigger UB) may be rejected at HIR typecheck in a future milestone.

## §3.1.13 M3.1 — Cooperative-matrix dispatch + multi-row matmul + GPU-resident benchmark

M3.1 makes the M2.1 cooperative-matrix codegen EXECUTE on real tensor cores for the first time, adds a multi-row Q4_K_M matmul, and builds the GPU-resident benchmark methodology. The emitted coopmat SPIR-V is UNCHANGED from M2.1; M3.1 is a RUNTIME + metadata + kernel + benchmark milestone. The only codegen touch is a mechanical internal-cache-key extension (CoopMatKey gains K + result_type) that leaves the emitted module byte-identical.

### Coopmat shape metadata (HIR → sidecar → runtime)

Previously the runtime had no way to know a kernel used cooperative matrices or what shape it required. M3.1 plumbs the shape end-to-end. The HIR derives a kernel-level `CoopMatShape{m,n,k,a/b/c/result element types, scope}` from the body's `matrix[T,M,N,use]` types of the coopmat ops (reusing M2.1 typecheck guarantees, so K = a.n = b.m). The metadata sidecar gains a `coopmat: Option<CoopMatShapeMeta>` field; `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` is bumped 1→2 and `load_kernel_metadata` accepts both versions (v1 sidecar with no field deserializes to `None` via `#[serde(default)]`, dispatch-identical to non-coopmat). The runtime builds the required shape FROM the metadata — nothing hardcoded.

### Device-feature enablement: the capability→feature pass

spirv-val validates a module but does NOT check that the device features its capabilities require are enabled. M3.1 adds a binding-plan-driven `required_device_features` pass:

| SPIR-V capability | trigger | Vulkan device feature |
|---|---|---|
| VulkanMemoryModel | coopmat op | VkPhysicalDeviceVulkanMemoryModelFeatures.vulkanMemoryModel (+DeviceScope) |
| CooperativeMatrixKHR | coopmat op | VK_KHR_cooperative_matrix ext + cooperativeMatrix feature |
| StorageBuffer16BitAccess | f16 SSBO | storageBuffer16BitAccess |
| StorageBuffer8BitAccess | u8/i8 SSBO | storageBuffer8BitAccess |
| Int8 | u8/i8 SSBO | shaderInt8 |
| Int16 | f16_bits_to_f32 | shaderInt16 |

The runtime enables the device-SUPPORTED subset at context creation (all feature-struct locals at function scope so they outlive `create_device`, chain assembled unconditionally), and a kernel that needs a feature the device lacks FAILS CLOSED with `DeviceFeatureUnsupported` — never enable-and-hope.

### Preflight, subgroup guard, graceful skip

A preflight queries `vkGetPhysicalDeviceCooperativeMatrixPropertiesKHR` for the device's supported `(M,N,K,AType,BType,CType,ResultType,scope,saturatingAccumulation)` tuples. A pure matcher checks the metadata-derived required shape against that set. matmul_tile requires `subgroupSize == 32`; wave64/SIMD16 devices are SKIPPED (not miscomputed). When unsupported, dispatch returns `DispatchError::CoopMatUnsupported`. The NVIDIA runner is a mandatory sign-off gate.

### Multi-row Q4_K_M matmul and staged coopmat bridge

`q4km_dequant_matmul.axc` extends M2.6's single-row matvec to N-row × M-col, bit-exact vs CPU reference for 256×256 on Lavapipe AND NVIDIA. A pre-dequantized coopmat bridge (`q4km_dequant_matmul_coopmat.axc`) is provided for NVIDIA-only proof without `shared[T,N]` (deferred to M3.2).

### GPU-resident benchmark methodology

upload_resident / dispatch_resident / readback_resident: inputs staged once, descriptor set bound to resident device-local buffers, each iteration records ONLY the compute submit timed by `vkCmdWriteTimestamp` × 2 (elapsed = (end−begin) · timestampPeriod, each endpoint masked to `timestampValidBits` before subtraction, CPU fallback). Effective TFLOPS = 2*M*N*K / kernel_seconds (the honest, reproducible in-tree number). The cuBLAS comparison is an external estimate, labeled as such.

### Lever A re-land

`binding_is_readback(access, output_size) = output_size > 0 && access != ReadOnly` applied to all four readback loops (HN-9 — all loops must agree). ReadOnly bindings skip the device→staging copy, HOST_READ barrier, and invalidate. Re-landed without the reverted ReBAR Lever B from M3.0.
